**But the first explanation's agreement with the parable as a whole may be recorded, and I give this as my own reading:** all the elements of the parable are things that **increase the quality of the light** — the niche gathers, the glass guards and concentrates, the olive burns clean. **And the qualification "neither of the east nor of the west" conveys, on the same line, the purity of the oil.**

**And the second explanation is worth noting too, because it looks at another side of the verse:** *nūr* is not the property of a region, of a lineage, of a party. **This agrees with the general language of the sūra and of the Qurʾān** — as was recorded at `049-hucurat.md`, 49/13: the measure is not lineage but *ʿinda'llāh*.

#### يَكَادُ زَيْتُهَا يُضِىٓءُ وَلَوْ لَمْ تَمْسَسْهُ نَارٌ

**This is the most striking clause of the parable.**

**يَكَادُ — "almost".** In Arabic the verb *kāda* conveys that a thing has come very close to happening but has not happened.

**And what the clause builds is this: the oil *comes close* to giving light without fire's touching it — but it does not give it.**

**Two notes are needed:**

**One. Fire is nonetheless required.** The verb *kāda* says exactly this: **almost**, but not. The oil does not give light by itself.

**Two. But the degree of readiness is stressed.** The oil is so pure that it stands as close to kindling as can be.

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the verb itself:** *kāda* conveys not the happening but the **nearness.** To read the clause as "the oil burns of itself" runs against the sense of the verb.

**تَمْسَسْهُ — root م-س-س:** to touch. **The same verb occurred in the fourteenth verse:** *la-massakum … ʿadhābun ʿaẓīm.* **In both places the lightest contact.**

**And the two occurrences build a contrast:**

| Verse | What touches | The outcome |
|---|---|---|
| **14** | *ʿadhābun ʿaẓīm* | A punishment would have touched them |
| **35** | *nār* — fire | It would give light even without its touching |

**The same verb, the one in a negative and the other in a positive context.** I record this as an observation about the word order.

**And the root of the word *nār* is the same as the sūra's name: ن-و-ر.** Fire and light, from one root. **And what the clause does is to separate the two:** light is (almost) possible without fire.

#### نُّورٌ عَلَىٰ نُورٍ — light upon light

**This is the seal of the parable, and it consists of two words.**

**In Arabic the expression conveys a *layering*: one light above another light.** And in the classical commentaries several explanations of what this is are transmitted:

| Explanation | Its content |
|---|---|
| **1** | The layers of the elements in the parable: the lights of the niche, the glass, the fire and the oil, one upon another |
| **2** | Guidances coming one after another: after one light, another light |
| **3** | The expression is an intensification conveying the **degree** of the light |

**I impose no preference.**

**And the contrast the expression will build with the fortieth verse will be taken up in that discussion:** there it will say *ẓulumātun baʿḍuhā fawqa baʿḍ* — "darknesses, one above another". **The same structure, with the opposite object.**

**And it may be recorded that the word *nūr* occurs five times in this verse:** *nūru'l-samāwāt*, *nūrihī*, *nūrun*, *ʿalā nūrin*, *li-nūrihī*. **The sūra's name, five times in the sūra's central verse.**

I record this as an observation of count.

### The elements of the parable — the whole table

**What each element does in the parable:**

| Element | Word | What | Its work in the parable |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **مِشْكَاة** (*mishkāt*) | The hollow in the wall, the lamp-niche | **It gathers** — it prevents the light's scattering, and directs it |
| **2** | **مِصْبَاح** (*miṣbāḥ*) | The lamp | **The source** — the body that produces the light |
| **3** | **زُجَاجَة** (*zujāja*) | The glass | **It protects and increases** — it guards the flame from the wind and lets the light through |
| **4** | **كَوْكَبٌ دُرِّىّ** | A star like a pearl | **It qualifies** — it conveys that the glass itself shines too |
| **5** | **شَجَرَة مُبَارَكَة زَيْتُونَة** | The blessed olive tree | **It feeds** — the source of the fuel; continuance and blessing |
| **6** | **لَا شَرْقِيَّة وَلَا غَرْبِيَّة** | Neither of the east nor of the west | **It purifies** — it fixes the quality of the oil; and a belonging to nowhere |
| **7** | **يَكَادُ زَيْتُهَا يُضِىء** | Oil that would almost give light | **Readiness** — the state closest to kindling |
| **8** | **نُورٌ عَلَىٰ نُور** | Light upon light | **Layers** — the seal of the parable |

**And what the table shows is this, which I record as my own reading:**

**Seven of the eight elements in the parable are not the light *itself* — they are the apparatus around the light.** The niche, the glass, the tree, the oil, the direction, the readiness: all of them are elements that make the light possible, that guard it, feed it and increase it.

**The parable, that is, tells not how a light comes to be but how a light is *kept*.**

**And this agrees with the sūra as a whole.** The whole subject of Sūrat al-Nūr is protection and boundary: the guarding of standing (4-5, 11-26), the guarding of the house (27-29, 58-59), the guarding of the glance and of chastity (30-31). **And the parable in the central verse tells how a flame is kept.**

**I record this connection as my own reading;** the functions of the elements in the parable are read from the wording of the verse.

### A note on the variant readings

**Different readings of the verb يُوقَدُ are recorded in the classical sources on the variant readings** — in the passive (*yūqadu*) and in active forms, and also with a feminine pronoun (*tūqadu*). **It is recorded that they convey no great change in meaning. Since I cannot state with certainty which reader read which, I write no names.**

**There is a grammatical debate about the subject of the verb as well:** is it the *miṣbāḥ* that burns, or the *zujāja*? **This is a question of case arising from the reference of the pronouns in the sentence, and it is debated in the classical sources.** I make no preference.

### يَهْدِى ٱللَّهُ لِنُورِهِۦ مَن يَشَآءُ

**The clause that comes after the parable says whose the parable is.**

**And the particle is worth noting: *yahdī li-nūrihī* — "He guides to His light".** Not *ilā* but the *lām*. The classical lexicographers record a fine difference between the use of these two particles with the verb of guiding; **since I cannot give a definite rule, I draw no conclusion.**

**And the verb of the clause is worth noting: *yahdī* — to guide, to show the way.**

**The distinction recorded at `002-bakara.md`, 2/257, works here:**

> **ٱلظُّلُمَٰت is plural, ٱلنُّور singular. And this is a consistent usage in the Qurʾān.** … *"darkness is the absence of light, and absence has many shapes; light is one. The way is lost in more than one manner, but the place arrived at is one."*

**And Sūrat al-Nūr repeats this distinction within itself:**

| Verse | Word | Number |
|---|---|---|
| **35** | *nūr* | **Singular** — five times, always singular |
| **40** | *ẓulumāt* | **Plural** — and *baʿḍuhā fawqa baʿḍ* |
| **40** | *nūr* | **Singular** — *fa-mā lahū min nūr* |

**The pattern recorded in al-Baqara is confirmed twice in Sūrat al-Nūr, five verses apart.** I record this as an observation about the word order.

### وَيَضْرِبُ ٱللَّهُ ٱلْأَمْثَٰلَ لِلنَّاسِ — the parable being given its name

**Having built the parable, the verse gives it a name: *amthāl* — likenesses.**

**And this is the most important note within the verse itself, and the text here repeats the note of *tanzīh* entered at the head of this section:** what is described is a **likeness.**

**The idiom *ḍaraba mathal* — "to strike a likeness" — conveys in Arabic the framing of a comparison.** The root ض-ر-ب occurred twice in the thirty-first verse of this sūra (*yaḍribna bi-khumurihinna*, *lā yaḍribna bi-arjulihinna*). **The same verb, in three distinct tasks.**

**And the qualification *li'l-nās* is worth noting:** the likeness is given not to a particular group but **to people.** This is one of the few places in the sūra where the word *nās* occurs.

---

## 24/36-38 — فِى بُيُوتٍ أَذِنَ ٱللَّهُ أَن تُرْفَعَ وَيُذْكَرَ فِيهَا ٱسْمُهُۥ يُسَبِّحُ لَهُۥ فِيهَا بِٱلْغُدُوِّ وَٱلْءَاصَالِ · رِجَالٌ لَّا تُلْهِيهِمْ تِجَٰرَةٌ وَلَا بَيْعٌ عَن ذِكْرِ ٱللَّهِ وَإِقَامِ ٱلصَّلَوٰةِ وَإِيتَآءِ ٱلزَّكَوٰةِ يَخَافُونَ يَوْمًا تَتَقَلَّبُ فِيهِ ٱلْقُلُوبُ وَٱلْأَبْصَٰرُ · لِيَجْزِيَهُمُ ٱللَّهُ أَحْسَنَ مَا عَمِلُوا۟ وَيَزِيدَهُم مِّن فَضْلِهِۦ وَٱللَّهُ يَرْزُقُ مَن يَشَآءُ بِغَيْرِ حِسَابٍ

*Fī buyūtin adhina'llāhu an turfaʿa wa yudhkara fīha'smuh, yusabbiḥu lahū fīhā bi'l-ghuduwwi wa'l-āṣāl · Rijālun lā tulhīhim tijāratun wa lā bayʿun ʿan dhikri'llāhi wa iqāmi'l-ṣalāti wa ītāʾi'l-zakāh, yakhāfūna yawman tataqallabu fīhi'l-qulūbu wa'l-abṣār · Li-yajziyahumu'llāhu aḥsana mā ʿamilū wa yazīdahum min faḍlih, wa'llāhu yarzuqu man yashāʾu bi-ghayri ḥisāb*

"(That lamp is) in houses God has given leave to be raised, and that His name be mentioned in them. In them He is glorified morning and evening by men whom neither trade nor commerce turns away from the remembrance of God, from the keeping up of the prayer and the giving of the alms. They fear a day on which hearts and eyes will be turned about — that God may reward them for the best of what they did, and increase them of His bounty. God provides for whom He wills without reckoning."

### فِى بُيُوتٍ — the houses, and the ring within the sūra

**The verse says where the lamp of the parable is: *fī buyūt* — "in houses".**

**And the word had occurred seven verses earlier in an altogether different task.**

| Verse | *Bayt* | What is done |
|---|---|---|
| **27** | *buyūtan ghayra buyūtikum* | **Not entered without leave** |
| **29** | *buyūtan ghayra maskūna* | The exception |
| **36** | *fī buyūtin adhina'llāh* | **Houses God has given leave for** |
| **61** | *buyūtikum … buyūti ābāʾikum …* | The manners of eating |

**And the wording in the thirty-sixth verse is worth noting: *adhina'llāhu* — "God gave leave".**

**The word for leave (أ-ذ-ن), that is, occurs in two distinct places in the sūra, and in both it has to do with a house:**

| Verse | Who gives the leave | What is allowed |
|---|---|---|
| **28** | **The householder** | Entering |
| **36** | **God** | The **raising** of the house |

**I record this ring as my own reading;** the two occurrences of the root and their subjects stand in the text.

### أَن تُرْفَعَ — being raised

**رَفَعَ — root ر-ف-ع:** to raise. The root was analysed at `049-hucurat.md`, 49/2 (*lā tarfaʿū aṣwātakum*).

**And there is a contrast between the two usages that is worth noting:**

| Verse | What is raised | The ruling |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Ḥujurāt 49/2** | *aṣwātakum* — voices | **Forbidden** |
| **al-Nūr 24/36** | *buyūt* — houses | **Permitted** |

**The same verb, the one forbidden and the other permitted.** I record this as an observation about the word order.

**And two explanations of what the verb *turfaʿa* means are transmitted in the classical commentaries:**

| Explanation | Its content |
|---|---|
| **1. Physical** | The building of them, their construction |
| **2. Spiritual** | Their being honoured, kept clean, held in esteem |

**The two explanations do not exclude one another, and I impose no preference.**

### رِجَالٌ لَّا تُلْهِيهِمْ تِجَٰرَةٌ وَلَا بَيْعٌ

**أَلْهَىٰ — root ل-ه-و:** to be diverted, to have one's attention drawn elsewhere. *Lahw* — what diverts. The root was analysed at `102-tekasur.md`, 102/1 (*alhākumu'l-takāthur*).

**And the choice of the verb is worth noting:** the verse does not say "they do not trade". **It says "trade does not turn them away".**

**What is censured, that is, is not trade but trade's *holding the attention*.** This distinction is read from the text and agrees with the measure recorded at `062-cuma.md`, 62/11.

**The difference between تِجَارَة and بَيْع is recorded in the classical commentaries thus:** *tijāra* is a continuing occupation, *bayʿ* a single transaction of buying and selling. **The verse, that is, names both the continuing and the momentary.**

I transmit this distinction as an explanation that is transmitted.

### يَوْمًا تَتَقَلَّبُ فِيهِ ٱلْقُلُوبُ وَٱلْأَبْصَٰرُ

**تَقَلَّبَ — root ق-ل-ب:** to turn, to turn upside down. **And from the same root comes *qalb* (the heart)** — the connection the lexicographers draw: the heart is what turns continually from state to state.

**In the verse two derivatives of the root stand side by side: *tataqallabu* and *al-qulūb*.** A *jinās* (the standing together of words from one root) is formed.

**And the word *al-abṣār* forms a ring with the glance of the thirtieth and thirty-first verses:**

| Verse | *Baṣar* | What happens |
|---|---|---|
| **30-31** | *yaghuḍḍū min abṣārihim* | **Man** lowers his glance |
| **37** | *tataqallabu fīhi … al-abṣār* | **The eyes** are turned about — not in man's hands |

**The sūra first records man's will over the eye, then names the place where that will ends.**

**I record this as my own reading;** the places of the words stand in the text.

### لِيَجْزِيَهُمُ ٱللَّهُ أَحْسَنَ مَا عَمِلُوا۟

**The expression is worth noting: *aḥsana mā ʿamilū* — "the best of what they did".**

**The recompense, that is, is given not according to the average of what was done but according to the best of it.** And on top of that an increase is added: *wa yazīdahum min faḍlih.*

**بِغَيْرِ حِسَابٍ** — "without reckoning". The word can be understood in two ways, and both are transmitted in the classical commentaries: **without measure, without limit**, or **without being brought to account.** I impose no preference.

---

## 24/39 — وَٱلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوٓا۟ أَعْمَٰلُهُمْ كَسَرَابٍۭ بِقِيعَةٍ يَحْسَبُهُ ٱلظَّمْـَٔانُ مَآءً حَتَّىٰٓ إِذَا جَآءَهُۥ لَمْ يَجِدْهُ شَيْـًٔا وَوَجَدَ ٱللَّهَ عِندَهُۥ فَوَفَّىٰهُ حِسَابَهُۥ وَٱللَّهُ سَرِيعُ ٱلْحِسَابِ

*Wa'lladhīna kafarū aʿmāluhum ka-sarābin bi-qīʿatin yaḥsabuhu'l-ẓamʾānu māʾan ḥattā idhā jāʾahū lam yajidhu shayʾan wa wajada'llāha ʿindahū fa-waffāhu ḥisābah, wa'llāhu sarīʿu'l-ḥisāb*

"And the deeds of those who disbelieve are like a mirage on a level plain: the thirsty man takes it for water; when he comes to it he finds it nothing at all. But he finds God there, and God pays him his account in full. God is swift in reckoning."

### The counter-parable begins

**In the thirty-fifth verse a parable of light was built. The thirty-ninth and fortieth verses build its counter-parables.**

**And the arrangement is worth noting: there are two counter-parables, not one.**

| Verse | Parable | Its subject |
|---|---|---|
| **35** | The lamp | **Light** |
| **39** | **The mirage** | What appears but is not |
| **40** | **Darkness upon darkness** | What is not seen |

**Two distinct things, that is, are set against the light, and the two differ from each other.** This is the most important observation about the arrangement in this section, and it will be taken up with a table at the fortieth verse.

### سَرَاب — the mirage

**Root: س-ر-ب.** This root was analysed at `078-nebe.md`, 78/20 (*fa-kānat sarābā*); I rely on that treatment. The note there:

> **سَرَاب — Root: س-ر-ب.** The concrete sense of the root is **to flow, to go, to set out upon a road.** … **سَرَاب** — a mirage: the appearance that looks like water from afar in the heat and vanishes when one draws near.

**And the idea of "flowing" in the root of the word explains the look of the mirage:** a mirage looks from a distance like **running water.**

**The physical cause of a mirage is a known phenomenon:** the layer of air above a heated ground surface bends the light and reflects the sky upon the ground. **This is a note of information; no scientific claim is loaded onto the verse.**

### بِقِيعَةٍ — level ground

**قِيعَة / قَاع — root ق-و-ع.** The sense the lexicographers record: **level, wide ground with no plants upon it.** *Qāʿ* — a flat.

**And the word occurs once more in the Qurʾān:** *fa-yadharuhā qāʿan ṣafṣafā* (Ṭāhā 20/106) — "He will leave it a level plain".

**The work of the word here is worth noting:** a mirage is seen only on **level and empty** ground. It does not form on broken ground or where there are plants.

**The verse, that is, does not merely say "a mirage" — it names the ground on which the mirage forms.**

**I record this as my own reading and add this:** this element of the parable builds a contrast with the parable of the thirty-fifth verse. **There the ground was an olive tree — rooted, long-lived, fruitful. Here the ground is a *qīʿa* — an empty flat.**

| | 24/35 | 24/39 |
|---|---|---|
| **The ground** | *shajaratun mubāraka* — a blessed tree | *qīʿa* — an empty flat |
| **The source** | *zayt* — oil, real fuel | **None** — an appearance |
| **The outcome** | *nūrun ʿalā nūr* | *lam yajidhu shayʾā* |

### ٱلظَّمْـَٔان — the thirsty man

**Root: ظ-م-أ.** Its concrete sense is **thirst.** *Ẓamaʾ* — thirst; *ẓamʾān* — one who is thirsty.

**The word occurs in several places in the Qurʾān:** *lā taẓmaʾu fīhā wa lā taḍḥā* (Ṭāhā 20/119) — "there you will neither thirst nor be exposed to the sun".

**And the work of the word here is the most affecting side of the parable, which I record as my own reading:**

**The man who sees the mirage is a thirsty man.** And thirst makes the deception both possible and intelligible. **A man is inclined to see the thing he is looking for.**

**The parable, that is, names not the folly of the deceived but his *need*.** This gives the parable not harshness but realism.

**And the verb is worth noting: *yaḥsabuhū* — "he takes it for".** The same verb occurred in the sūra at the eleventh verse (*lā taḥsabūhu sharran*) and at the fifteenth (*taḥsabūnahū hayyinan*).

| Verse | *Ḥasiba* — to take a thing for | What |
|---|---|---|
| **11** | *lā taḥsabūhu sharran lakum* | To take the slander for an evil |
| **15** | *taḥsabūnahū hayyinā* | **To take it for a small thing** |
| **39** | *yaḥsabuhu'l-ẓamʾānu māʾan* | **To take a mirage for water** |
| **57** | *lā taḥsabanna'lladhīna kafarū muʿjizīn* | To take them for able to thwart |

**The root four times in the sūra, and in every one of them it conveys a *wrong appraisal*.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order;** the places of the words can be counted in the text. **And the diagnosis of the fifteenth verse — the taking of a thing for *hayyin* — is here pictured in a parable.**

### وَوَجَدَ ٱللَّهَ عِندَهُۥ — an unlooked-for clause

**The arrangement of the clause is worth noting: *lam yajidhu shayʾan wa wajada'llāha ʿindah*.**

**The same verb twice: *lam yajid* (he did not find) and *wajada* (he found).**

| | What was sought | What was found |
|---|---|---|
| **The first** | Water | **Nothing at all** |
| **The second** | — | **God** |

**What was sought was not there; but something not sought was.**

**This is the most unlooked-for move in the parable, and I record it as my own reading:** the mirage was empty, but the place where the mirage was was not empty. **The outcome of the deception is not a nothingness but an encounter.**

### فَوَفَّىٰهُ حِسَابَهُۥ

**وَفَّىٰ — root و-ف-ي:** analysed at the twenty-fifth verse. **To pay in full.**

**And the word occurred in the twenty-fifth verse too: *yuwaffīhimu'llāhu dīnahumu'l-ḥaqq*.** In both places the same verb, in the same task.

---

## 24/40 — أَوْ كَظُلُمَٰتٍ فِى بَحْرٍ لُّجِّىٍّ يَغْشَىٰهُ مَوْجٌ مِّن فَوْقِهِۦ مَوْجٌ مِّن فَوْقِهِۦ سَحَابٌ ظُلُمَٰتٌۢ بَعْضُهَا فَوْقَ بَعْضٍ إِذَآ أَخْرَجَ يَدَهُۥ لَمْ يَكَدْ يَرَىٰهَا وَمَن لَّمْ يَجْعَلِ ٱللَّهُ لَهُۥ نُورًا فَمَا لَهُۥ مِن نُّورٍ

*Aw ka-ẓulumātin fī baḥrin lujjiyyin yaghshāhu mawjun min fawqihī mawjun min fawqihī saḥāb, ẓulumātun baʿḍuhā fawqa baʿḍ, idhā akhraja yadahū lam yakad yarāhā, wa man lam yajʿali'llāhu lahū nūran fa-mā lahū min nūr*

"Or like darknesses in a deep sea, covered by a wave, above it another wave, above that a cloud: darknesses one above another. If he puts out his hand he can hardly see it. And whomever God has given no light — for him there is no light at all."

### The second counter-parable

**The thirty-ninth verse described a *mirage*; the fortieth describes *ẓulumāt*. And the two parables take up different things.**

**Recording this difference is the most important work of this section, and I give it as my own reading:**

| | **24/39 — The mirage** | **24/40 — The darkness** |
|---|---|---|
| **What is there** | **An appearance, but no thing** | **Neither appearance nor thing** |
| **Deception** | **Present** — it is taken for water | **Absent** — nothing is seen at all |
| **The man's condition** | **Hopeful** — he is running towards it | **Helpless** — he cannot see even his hand |
| **The place** | **The desert** — dry, level, lit | **The bottom of the sea** — watery, deep, dark |
| **The direction** | **Horizontal** — towards a distance | **Vertical** — layers one upon another |
| **When it is understood** | **On arrival** (*ḥattā idhā jāʾahū*) | **Never** — there is nothing to understand |

**The two parables tell of two distinct conditions:** the one of **believing a wrong thing**, the other of **being unable to see anything at all.**

**And both stand against the thirty-fifth verse — but from different directions:**

| | 24/35 — The light | 24/39 — The mirage | 24/40 — The darkness |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Is there light** | **Yes** | **Yes, but deceiving** (sunlight produces the mirage) | **No** |
| **Layers** | *nūrun ʿalā nūr* — **increasing** | A single layer | *ẓulumātun baʿḍuhā fawqa baʿḍ* — **diminishing** |
| **The work of the layers** | They **guard and multiply** the light | — | They **cut off** the light |
| **The outcome** | Seeing | Arriving at nothing | **Not even seeing one's hand** |

**The structure of layers in the two parables is the most striking place in the third line:**

**In the thirty-fifth verse the layers were set one upon another, and each layer increased the light:** the niche, the glass, the oil, the fire — all coming together to make *nūrun ʿalā nūr*.

**In the fortieth verse the layers are also set one upon another, and each layer increases the darkness:** the sea, the wave, the second wave, the cloud — all coming together to make *ẓulumātun baʿḍuhā fawqa baʿḍ*.

**The same structure, two opposite outcomes.** And the two expressions are each other's reflection in a mirror:

| Verse | Expression |
|---|---|
| **35** | *نُّورٌ عَلَىٰ نُورٍ* — **light upon light** |
| **40** | *ظُلُمَٰتٌۢ بَعْضُهَا فَوْقَ بَعْضٍ* — **darknesses, one above another** |

**I record this contrast as my own reading;** the structure and the places of the two expressions are the wording of the text and can be compared.

### لُّجِّىّ — the deep sea

**Root: ل-ج-ج.** The sense the lexicographers record: **to persist in a thing, to press upon it; and water's being much and deep.**

*Lujj* — the vast and deep part of the sea. *Lajja* — he persisted. *Lijāj* — obstinacy.

**And the connection between the two senses of the root is drawn by the lexicographers through a shared idea: a thing's piling up one upon another, its increase.** Persistence is the piling of an act upon itself; a *lujja* is the piling of water upon itself.

**The Qurʾān uses the same root in another place:** not in the passage *ṣarḥun mumarradun min qawārīr*, but the word *lujja* occurs at al-Naml 27/44.

**Its work in the parable:** depth. **The distance from the surface.**

### يَغْشَىٰهُ مَوْجٌ — the covering wave

**غَشِيَ — root غ-ش-ي:** to cover, to overspread, to envelop. *Ghishāwa* — the film over the eye. The root was taken up at `053-necm.md`, 53/16 (*idh yaghsha'l-sidrata mā yaghshā*).

**مَوْج — root م-و-ج:** to surge, to move in waves.

**And the counting of the layers is worth noting. The verse enumerates four layers:**

| Layer | Expression |
|---|---|
| **1** | *baḥrin lujjiyy* — the deep sea itself |
| **2** | *yaghshāhu mawj* — a wave |
| **3** | *min fawqihī mawj* — above it another wave |
| **4** | *min fawqihī saḥāb* — above that a cloud |

**And all four do the same thing: they come in between the source of the light and the man.**

**And the fourth layer is worth noting: *saḥāb* — a cloud.** The root **س-ح-ب**: to drag, to draw. **The root was analysed at `052-tur.md`, 52/44:** *"a cloud is what is dragged across the sky."*

**And the cloud will return in the sūra's forty-third verse — but there in an altogether different task: as the source of the rain.**

| Verse | *Saḥāb* | Its function |
|---|---|---|
| **40** | *min fawqihī saḥāb* | **The last layer of the darkness** |
| **43** | *yuzjī saḥāban* | **The source of the rain** |

**The same word, three verses apart, in two opposite tasks.** I record this as an observation about the word order.

### إِذَآ أَخْرَجَ يَدَهُۥ لَمْ يَكَدْ يَرَىٰهَا

**The last clause of the parable gives the degree of the darkness by a measure: the man cannot see his own hand.**

**And the choice of the measure is worth noting, and I record this as my own reading:** the verse names not something far off but **the nearest** thing. **The measure of darkness is the invisibility not of the far but of the near.**

**And the verb is the same as the verb of the thirty-fifth verse: *yakādu* / *lam yakad*.**

| Verse | *Kāda* | What |
|---|---|---|
| **35** | *yakādu zaytuhā yuḍīʾu* | **It would almost give light** — positive |
| **40** | *lam yakad yarāhā* | **He can hardly see it** — negative |

**The same verb, the one positive and the other negative, and both conveying an approach to a threshold.**

**I record this contrast as my own reading;** the two occurrences of the verb stand in the text.

### وَمَن لَّمْ يَجْعَلِ ٱللَّهُ لَهُۥ نُورًا فَمَا لَهُۥ مِن نُّورٍ

**The seal of the section, and the last occurrence of the root *nūr*.**

**The structure of the sentence is a condition and its answer, and the arrangement is worth noting: *nūr* occurs twice** — the one indefinite (*nūran*), the other an indefinite reinforced with *min* (*min nūrin*).

**In Arabic the pattern *mā … min* makes a negation absolute:** "there is no light at all". *Min* is here the particle that generalises the negation (*min zāʾida li'l-tanzīs*).

**And the contrast with the thirty-fifth verse is completed:**

| Verse | Clause |
|---|---|
| **35** | *yahdi'llāhu **li-nūrihī** man yashāʾ* — He guides whom He wills to His light |
| **40** | *wa man lam yajʿali'llāhu lahū **nūran** fa-mā lahū min nūr* — whomever God has given no light has no light |

**The two clauses carry the same structure and are tied to the same source.**

**And the pattern of plural and singular recorded at `002-bakara.md`, 2/257, is confirmed again here:** in the verse *ẓulumāt* is plural (twice), *nūr* singular (twice).

---

## 24/41-42 — أَلَمْ تَرَ أَنَّ ٱللَّهَ يُسَبِّحُ لَهُۥ مَن فِى ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ وَٱلطَّيْرُ صَٰٓفَّٰتٍ كُلٌّ قَدْ عَلِمَ صَلَاتَهُۥ وَتَسْبِيحَهُۥ وَٱللَّهُ عَلِيمٌۢ بِمَا يَفْعَلُونَ · وَلِلَّهِ مُلْكُ ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ وَإِلَى ٱللَّهِ ٱلْمَصِيرُ

*A-lam tara anna'llāha yusabbiḥu lahū man fi'l-samāwāti wa'l-arḍi wa'l-ṭayru ṣāffāt, kullun qad ʿalima ṣalātahū wa tasbīḥah, wa'llāhu ʿalīmun bimā yafʿalūn · Wa li'llāhi mulku'l-samāwāti wa'l-arḍ, wa ila'llāhi'l-maṣīr*

"Have you not seen that God is glorified by whoever is in the heavens and the earth, and by **the birds with wings outspread**? Each knows its own prayer and its own glorification. God knows what they do. To God belongs the dominion of the heavens and the earth; and to God is the journey's end."

### أَلَمْ تَرَ — a new section of the sūra

**With the forty-first verse the sūra enters a new series, and the series is marked by a formula: *a-lam tara* — "have you not seen?"**

**And the formula occurs three times in the sūra: at 41, at 43, and at 44 (indirectly, as *inna fī dhālika*).**

**The function of the formula is worth noting:** what looks like a question is in Arabic a way of **drawing attention.** No information is being asked of the addressee; he is being asked to look at something he already knows.

**And that the verb is *raʾā* (to see) agrees entirely with the section that follows the thirty-fifth verse.** The sūra was built upon the glance:

| Verse | The verb of seeing |
|---|---|
| **30-31** | *yaghuḍḍū min abṣārihim* — lower the glance |
| **35** | The parable of light |
| **40** | *lam yakad yarāhā* — he cannot see |
| **41, 43** | ***a-lam tara*** — have you not seen? |
| **44** | *li-uli'l-abṣār* — for those who have eyes |

**The sūra first narrows the glance, then shows what is to be looked at, then asks "have you not seen?"**

**I record this as my own reading;** the places of the verbs stand in the text and can be counted.

### وَٱلطَّيْرُ صَٰٓفَّٰتٍ — the birds in their ranks

**صَٰٓفَّٰت — root ص-ف-ف.** This root was analysed at `037-saffat.md`, 37/1, and in `061-saff.md`; I rely on those treatments.

**The note in al-Ṣāffāt:**

> **The root was analysed in `061-saff.md`.** The definition there: **to set several things side by side upon a straight line, in one row.** … here the word comes as **a feminine plural active participle** — *ṣāffāt*. That is, not "those that are ranged" but **"those that range, those that form ranks"**. The verb is active.

**And at al-Nūr 24/41 the same word occurs in the same form: *ṣāffāt*.**

**But there is a difference, and it is worth noting:** in Sūrat al-Ṣāffāt the subject of the word is **not stated** — it comes as an element of an oath, and who it is that forms the ranks is not specified (in the hundred and sixty-fifth verse of that sūra the angels speak).

**In al-Nūr the subject is named outright: *al-ṭayr* — the birds.**

| Sūra | Expression | Subject |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Ṣāffāt 37/1** | *wa'l-ṣāffāti ṣaffā* | **Not stated** |
| **al-Nūr 24/41** | *wa'l-ṭayru **ṣāffāt*** | **The birds** |

**I record this as an observation about the word order.**

**And the word's use of the birds can be understood in two ways, and both are transmitted in the classical commentaries:**

| Explanation | Its content |
|---|---|
| **1** | The state in which birds **spread and hold out their wings** in flight — the wing forming a straight line |
| **2** | The birds' flying in **rows** across the sky |

**Both agree with the sense of the root** and do not exclude one another. I impose no preference.

**And it may be recorded that the word is used of birds in one other place in the Qurʾān:** *a-wa-lam yaraw ila'l-ṭayri fawqahum **ṣāffātin** wa yaqbiḍn* (al-Mulk 67/19) — treated in `067-mulk.md`. **There too the same word, in the same form, and of birds.**

### كُلٌّ قَدْ عَلِمَ صَلَاتَهُۥ وَتَسْبِيحَهُۥ — the clause that is disputed

**There is disagreement in the classical commentaries over the subject of the clause, and it needs transmitting.**

| Reading | The subject of the verb *ʿalima* | Its sense |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | ***kullun*** — every creature | Every creature knows **its own** prayer and glorification |
| **2** | **God** | God knows the prayer and glorification of each |

**Both readings are transmitted in the classical sources; I impose no preference, and neither is binding.**

**But the place of the second clause in the arrangement may be recorded:** immediately after, the verse says *wa'llāhu ʿalīmun bimā yafʿalūn*. **God's knowing, that is, is stated separately and openly.** This may provide a ground for the first reading — but I give it not as a preference but as an observation about the word order.

**And what the first reading carries is worth noting:** that every creature has a form of glorification **of its own.** Glorification, that is, is not of one type.

### يُسَبِّحُ — glorification

**Root: س-ب-ح.** Its concrete sense is **to swim in water or in air, to go flowing.** *Sabḥ* — swimming. The root has been taken up at many places in this commentary.

**And *tasbīḥ*: to keep a thing clear of what does not befit it.** The connection the lexicographers draw: what swims flows on, passing obstacles; and glorification likewise **removes** from the divine essence the ascriptions that do not befit it.

**The word occurs twice in the sūra: at 36 and at 41.** And at 36 it is those who glorify within houses, at 41 the heavens, the earth and the birds. **There is a widening between the two verses: from a house to the whole of existence.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order.**

**And the *subḥānaka* of the sixteenth verse is from the same root.** The root occurs three times in the sūra: at 16, 36 and 41.

### وَإِلَى ٱللَّهِ ٱلْمَصِيرُ

**مَصِير — root ص-ي-ر:** to become, to be transformed, to arrive at a state. *Maṣīr* — the place one arrives at, the place of return.

**And the sense of "becoming" in the root of the word is worth noting:** *maṣīr* is not merely a place — it is **an arriving at a state.**

---

## 24/43 — أَلَمْ تَرَ أَنَّ ٱللَّهَ يُزْجِى سَحَابًا ثُمَّ يُؤَلِّفُ بَيْنَهُۥ ثُمَّ يَجْعَلُهُۥ رُكَامًا فَتَرَى ٱلْوَدْقَ يَخْرُجُ مِنْ خِلَٰلِهِۦ وَيُنَزِّلُ مِنَ ٱلسَّمَآءِ مِن جِبَالٍ فِيهَا مِنۢ بَرَدٍ فَيُصِيبُ بِهِۦ مَن يَشَآءُ وَيَصْرِفُهُۥ عَن مَّن يَشَآءُ يَكَادُ سَنَا بَرْقِهِۦ يَذْهَبُ بِٱلْأَبْصَٰرِ

*A-lam tara anna'llāha yuzjī saḥāban thumma yuʾallifu baynahū thumma yajʿaluhū rukāman fa-tara'l-wadqa yakhruju min khilālih, wa yunazzilu mina'l-samāʾi min jibālin fīhā min baradin fa-yuṣību bihī man yashāʾu wa yaṣrifuhū ʿan man yashāʾ, yakādu sanā barqihī yadhhabu bi'l-abṣār*

"Have you not seen that God drives the clouds, then joins them together, then piles them up? Then you see the rain issuing from their midst. And He sends down from the sky, from mountains within it, hail; and He strikes with it whom He wills and turns it away from whom He wills. The flash of His lightning all but takes away the sight."

### يُزْجِى — to drive

**Root: ز-ج-و / ز-ج-ي.** The sense the lexicographers record: **to move a thing slowly, by pushing, by dragging it along.** *Izjāʾ* — to drive gently and set moving.

**And the fine point the word carries is this: the verb conveys not a forcible pushing but a *gentle driving forward*.**

**The Qurʾān uses the same root in two other places, and both bear out the sense:**

| Verse | Expression | Sense |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Isrāʾ 17/66** | *yuzjī lakumu'l-fulka fi'l-baḥr* | He **makes the ship move** upon the sea |
| **Yūsuf 12/88** | *biḍāʿatin **muzjāt*** | **A poor stock, one barely driven along** |

**The usage in Yūsuf gives the word's second sense: what is pushed along with difficulty, what is little and of small worth.**

**And the work of the usage in the verse is worth noting:** the clouds are driven — they do not go of themselves, but neither are they flung.

**I record this as an observation about the word.**

### Three stages: يُزْجِى → يُؤَلِّفُ → يَجْعَلُهُ رُكَامًا

**The verse sets the forming of the cloud in order with three verbs, and the verbs are joined by *thumma*.**

| Stage | Verb | Root | What is done |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | *yuzjī* | ز-ج-و | **To drive** — to set scattered clouds moving |
| **2** | *yuʾallifu baynahū* | أ-ل-ف | **To join** — to knit them together |
| **3** | *yajʿaluhū rukāman* | ر-ك-م | **To pile** — to set them one upon another |

**And the twofold use of the particle *thumma* conveys that there is time between the stages.** The process is not instantaneous but by degrees.

**أَلَّفَ — root أ-ل-ف:** **to knit together, to bring together and reconcile.** *Ulfa* — cohesion, becoming used to. *Alif* — the name of the letter; and *alf* — a thousand. The lexicographers record at the centre of the root the idea of **coming together and growing used to one another.**

**And that the word is used of human relations too is worth noting:** the Qurʾān says *allafa bayna qulūbihim* (Āl ʿImrān 3/103; al-Anfāl 8/63). **The same verb, for the joining both of clouds and of hearts.**

**I record this as an observation about the word.**

**رُكَام — root ر-ك-م.** This root was analysed at `052-tur.md`, 52/44; I rely on that treatment. The note there: **ر-ك-م: to pile one upon another. *Rukām* — a heap.**

**And the usage in al-Ṭūr is worth noting:** there *saḥābun markūm* — "a cloud piled up" — occurred, and it was in the form of a passive participle. **Here, however, *rukām* — in the verbal-noun form, and the second object of the verb *yajʿaluhū*.**

| Sūra | Expression | Form |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Ṭūr 52/44** | *saḥābun **markūm*** | A passive participle — **piled up** |
| **al-Nūr 24/43** | *yajʿaluhū **rukāmā*** | A verbal noun — **He makes it a heap** |

**The same root in both places, of a cloud.** I record this as an observation about the word order.

### ٱلْوَدْق — the drops of rain

**Root: و-د-ق.** This word was taken up in the table of words for rain in `042-sura.md`; I rely on that treatment. The note there:

> **وَدْق** (*wadq*) | و-د-ق | The **grains, the drops** of the rain

**And the table in the al-Shūrā section separated the words Arabic uses for rain:**

| Word | Root | What |
|---|---|---|
| **مَطَر** | م-ط-ر | The **event of raining itself** |
| **غَيْث** | غ-ي-ث | **Coming to the rescue** |
| **وَدْق** | و-د-ق | **Grains, drops** |
| **صَيِّب** | ص-و-ب | **A downpour that finds its mark** |

**And the choice of *wadq* agrees with the verse's way of looking:** the verse tells of the rain not as an event but as a **process** — the driving, the joining, the piling, and then the coming out of the drops.

**I record this as an observation about the word order.**

**مِنْ خِلَٰلِهِ — "from their midst".** *Khilāl* — root **خ-ل-ل**: the gap between two things, an interval. *Khalal* — a gap, a breach. **And *khalīl* (a friend) is from this root too** — the connection the lexicographers draw: friendship is that nothing else comes in between two people.

### مِن جِبَالٍ فِيهَا مِنۢ بَرَدٍ — the expression that is disputed

**This expression is the most disputed place in the verse in point of language, and several explanations are transmitted in the classical commentaries:**

| Explanation | Its content |
|---|---|
| **1** | *Jibāl* is a figure: the masses of cloud in the sky have been called "mountains" because of their size |
| **2** | The expression conveys the places in the sky from which the hail comes down; its how is not entered into |
| **3** | The *min* in the expression *min baradin* conveys the kind: heaps consisting of hail |

**I impose no preference, and it is not binding.**

**And a note of method needs entering:** no claim of "foretelling" modern meteorology is **drawn** from this verse. The rule of the method (`STYLE.md`) is plain: no hunting for scientific miracles.

**What can be said is this, and it is not a small thing:** the verse describes the forming of the rain as **a process by stages**, and by the particle *thumma* it conveys that there is time between the stages. **This stands within the text itself and needs no translating into a modern model.**

**The same measure was recorded in the discussion of `071-nuh.md`, 71/16**, and what was said there holds here too: to make a text need defending afresh with every new scientific model does not increase its force.

### يَكَادُ سَنَا بَرْقِهِۦ يَذْهَبُ بِٱلْأَبْصَٰرِ

**سَنَا — root س-ن-و/س-ن-ي:** a gleam, light. *Sanā* — the brightness of a thing.

**بَرْق — root ب-ر-ق:** lightning, a flash.

**And the verb occurs for the third time in the sūra: *yakādu*.**

| Verse | *Kāda* | What |
|---|---|---|
| **35** | *yakādu zaytuhā yuḍīʾu* | It would almost **give light** |
| **40** | *lam yakad yarāhā* | He can almost **not see** it |
| **43** | *yakādu sanā barqihī yadhhabu bi'l-abṣār* | It all but **takes away the sight** |

**In all three occurrences there is a matter of light, and all three approach a threshold:**

- **35:** the light comes close to being without fire.
- **40:** the eye comes close to seeing nothing at all.
- **43:** the light comes close to **destroying** sight.

**And the third is worth recording, and I record it as my own reading:** the sūra does not name light only as a good thing. **An excess of it also prevents seeing.**

**In the sūra's weave of light, that is, there are three positions:** the absence of light (40), the measure of light (35), and the excess of light (43). **And the sūra's name is the middle one.**

I record this reading as my own reading; the wording of the three verses stands in the text.

---

## 24/44-45 — يُقَلِّبُ ٱللَّهُ ٱلَّيْلَ وَٱلنَّهَارَ إِنَّ فِى ذَٰلِكَ لَعِبْرَةً لِّأُو۟لِى ٱلْأَبْصَٰرِ · وَٱللَّهُ خَلَقَ كُلَّ دَآبَّةٍ مِّن مَّآءٍ فَمِنْهُم مَّن يَمْشِى عَلَىٰ بَطْنِهِۦ وَمِنْهُم مَّن يَمْشِى عَلَىٰ رِجْلَيْنِ وَمِنْهُم مَّن يَمْشِى عَلَىٰٓ أَرْبَعٍ يَخْلُقُ ٱللَّهُ مَا يَشَآءُ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ عَلَىٰ كُلِّ شَىْءٍ قَدِيرٌ

*Yuqallibu'llāhu'l-layla wa'l-nahār, inna fī dhālika la-ʿibratan li-uli'l-abṣār · Wa'llāhu khalaqa kulla dābbatin min māʾ, fa-minhum man yamshī ʿalā baṭnihī wa minhum man yamshī ʿalā rijlayni wa minhum man yamshī ʿalā arbaʿ, yakhluqu'llāhu mā yashāʾ, inna'llāha ʿalā kulli shayʾin qadīr*

"God turns the night and the day about. In that there is a lesson for **those who have eyes.** God created every moving creature from water: some of them go upon their bellies, some upon two feet, and some upon four. God creates what He wills. God has power over everything."

### يُقَلِّبُ — to turn about

**The root ق-ل-ب was analysed at the thirty-seventh verse.** And there *tataqallabu'l-qulūbu wa'l-abṣār* — the turning about of hearts and eyes — was named.

**And here the same root comes in Form II, and as God's act: *yuqallibu*.**

| Verse | Form | Subject | Object |
|---|---|---|---|
| **37** | *tataqallabu* (Form V — reflexive) | Hearts and eyes | — |
| **44** | *yuqallibu* (Form II — transitive) | **God** | Night and day |

**The same root, the one a turning of itself, the other a turning of something else.** I record this as an observation about the word order.

### لِّأُو۟لِى ٱلْأَبْصَٰرِ — "those who have eyes"

**And the sūra's weave of the eye closes here.**

| Verse | *Baṣar* |
|---|---|
| **30-31** | *yaghuḍḍū min **abṣāri**him* — lower the glance |
| **37** | *tataqallabu … al-**abṣār*** — the eyes are turned about |
| **40** | *lam yakad yarāhā* — he cannot see |
| **43** | *yadhhabu bi'l-**abṣār*** — it takes away the sight |
| **44** | *li-uli'l-**abṣār*** — **for those who have eyes** |

**Five occurrences, the word five times, from one end of the sūra to the other.**

**And the last occurrence uses the word as a capacity: *ulu'l-abṣār* — "those who have eyes, those who can see".**

**This is of the same kind as the pattern recorded at `002-bakara.md`, 2/179:** there stood *ulu'l-albāb* (those who have pith), and this note was entered: *"All three measure not a capacity but a function."*

**Here it is the same: *ulu'l-abṣār* is not everyone who has an eye — it is the man who can look.**

**And this forms a ring with the command at the head of the sūra:** in the thirtieth verse the glance was **lowered**; in the forty-fourth those who can see are **named.**

**The sūra, that is, begins with a command that narrows the eye and arrives at a sentence that praises the man who sees.**

**I record this reading as my own reading;** the places of the words can be counted in the text.

**عِبْرَة — root ع-ب-ر:** its concrete sense is **to pass from one place to another, to cross over.** *ʿUbūr* — a crossing; *maʿbar* — a ford; *taʿbīr* — to interpret a dream (to pass from the image to the meaning); *ʿibāra* — the wording of a saying (the bridge over which the meaning is crossed).

**And *ʿibra*: to pass from what is seen to what is not seen.**

**The concrete sense of the root gives exactly the work of the word, and I record this as an observation about the root:** to take a lesson is not to stay within an event — it is **to pass from that event to somewhere else.**

### كُلَّ دَآبَّةٍ مِّن مَّآءٍ

**دَابَّة — root د-ب-ب:** to move upon the ground, to walk. *Dabba* — it walked. **And *dābba*: every living thing that moves upon the earth.**

**The scope of the word is wide: not only four-footed animals but every living thing that moves.** The threefold enumeration in the rest of the verse bears this out.

**مِّن مَّآءٍ** — "from water". **And a note is needed about the function of the particle *min*.**

**The particle can be understood in two ways, and both are transmitted in the classical commentaries:**

| Explanation | Its content |
|---|---|
| **1. It conveys a material** | The material from which living things were created is water |
| **2. It conveys a part (partitive)** | From part of the water; or the water of generation |

**I impose no preference.**

**And a note of method:** no claim of "foretelling" modern biology is **drawn** from this verse. The Qurʾān's naming of water as the source of life occurs in other places too (al-Anbiyāʾ 21/30), and this is an expression that stands within the text itself.

### Three ways of going

**The verse divides living creatures in three according to their manner of movement:**

| Order | Expression | What |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *ʿalā baṭnihī* | **Upon its belly** — the creeping things |
| **2** | *ʿalā rijlayn* | **Two feet** |
| **3** | *ʿalā arbaʿ* | **Four feet** |

**And the measure of the classification is worth noting: the number of feet — that is, the *manner of movement*.**

**The verse divides living creatures not by size, by usefulness, by where they live, or by kind — but by their walking.** And this agrees with the verse's own word: *dābba*, by its root, is "the one that walks".

**The verse, that is, classifies what it names by the measure of that name itself.**

**I record this as my own reading;** the root of the word and the measure of the classification stand in the text.

**And the clause *yakhluqu'llāhu mā yashāʾ* conveys that the enumeration is not closed.** The list is not limited to three; the verse leaves the list open itself.

---

## 24/46 — لَّقَدْ أَنزَلْنَآ ءَايَٰتٍ مُّبَيِّنَٰتٍ وَٱللَّهُ يَهْدِى مَن يَشَآءُ إِلَىٰ صِرَٰطٍ مُّسْتَقِيمٍ

*Laqad anzalnā āyātin mubayyināt, wa'llāhu yahdī man yashāʾu ilā ṣirāṭin mustaqīm*

"We have surely sent down clear verses. And God guides whom He wills to a straight path."

### The repetition of the thirty-fourth verse

**This verse is almost identical with the thirty-fourth, and the two verses frame a section.**

| Verse | Expression |
|---|---|
| **34** | *wa laqad anzalnā ilaykum āyātin mubayyināt…* |
| **46** | *laqad anzalnā āyātin mubayyināt…* |

**The section between the two verses (35-45) is the sūra's section of light and of the universe.** The sūra, that is, opens that section with a sentence and closes it with the same sentence.

**I record this as an observation about the word order;** the wording of the two verses stands in the text and can be compared.

**And the difference is worth noting: the thirty-fourth verse has *ilaykum* (to you), the forty-sixth does not.** And in the thirty-fourth three things were enumerated (*āyāt*, *mathal*, *mawʿiẓa*); here only one.

### وَٱللَّهُ يَهْدِى مَن يَشَآءُ إِلَىٰ صِرَٰطٍ مُّسْتَقِيمٍ

**The clause can be compared with the last clause of the thirty-fifth verse:**

| Verse | Expression | Particle |
|---|---|---|
| **35** | *yahdi'llāhu **li**-nūrihī man yashāʾ* | *lām* |
| **46** | *wa'llāhu yahdī man yashāʾu **ilā** ṣirāṭin mustaqīm* | *ilā* |

**The same verb, two different particles and two different objects: the one light, the other a road.**

**And reading the two objects together says something, which I record as my own reading:** light is not the road itself — it is **what makes the road visible.** The sūra names the two separately.

**صِرَاط — root ص-ر-ط.** The word was analysed in detail in the al-Fātiḥa section; I do not repeat it here.

**And that the word occurs only once in the sūra is worth noting:** although the sūra is a text of rulings and of manners, it uses the word "road" once — and that at the close.

---

## 24/47-50 — وَيَقُولُونَ ءَامَنَّا بِٱللَّهِ وَبِٱلرَّسُولِ وَأَطَعْنَا ثُمَّ يَتَوَلَّىٰ فَرِيقٌ مِّنْهُم مِّنۢ بَعْدِ ذَٰلِكَ … أَفِى قُلُوبِهِم مَّرَضٌ أَمِ ٱرْتَابُوٓا۟ أَمْ يَخَافُونَ أَن يَحِيفَ ٱللَّهُ عَلَيْهِمْ وَرَسُولُهُۥ بَلْ أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلظَّٰلِمُونَ

*Wa yaqūlūna āmannā bi'llāhi wa bi'l-rasūli wa aṭaʿnā thumma yatawallā farīqun minhum min baʿdi dhālik, wa mā ulāʾika bi'l-muʾminīn · Wa idhā duʿū ila'llāhi wa rasūlihī li-yaḥkuma baynahum idhā farīqun minhum muʿriḍūn · Wa in yakun lahumu'l-ḥaqqu yaʾtū ilayhi mudhʿinīn · A fī qulūbihim maraḍun ami'rtābū am yakhāfūna an yaḥīfa'llāhu ʿalayhim wa rasūluh, bal ulāʾika humu'l-ẓālimūn*

"They say, 'We believe in God and in the Messenger, and we obey'; then a party of them turns away after that. Those are not believers. When they are called to God and His Messenger, that he may judge between them, at once a party of them turns aside. But if the right is on their side they come to him submissively. Is there a sickness in their hearts, or have they fallen into doubt, or do they fear that God and His Messenger will wrong them? No — it is they who are the wrongdoers."

### The subject of the new block: the distance between word and deed

**With the forty-seventh verse the sūra enters a new block, and the subject of the block builds a link with the matter at the head of the sūra.**

**The verses describe a stance that says a word and does not do what the word requires.** And this lies in the same field as the diagnosis of the sūra's fifteenth verse: there too the distance between word and reality was the subject (*taqūlūna bi-afwāhikum mā laysa lakum bihī ʿilm*).

| Verse | The condition of the word |
|---|---|
| **15** | There is a word, **no knowledge** |
| **47** | There is a word, **no deed** |

**I record this as an observation about the word order.**

**And the same matter was taken up at `049-hucurat.md`, 49/14-17:** there a community had said "we have believed", and an answer had come. **The two sūras build the same structure — but in al-Nūr the measure is given by a *behaviour*: what a man does when he is called to judgement.**

### ٱلَّذِى بَيْنَ ٱلْقَوْلِ وَٱلْفِعْلِ — the test the verses set up

**The forty-eighth and forty-ninth verses set up a very concrete test, and this is the most striking side of the verses:**

| The situation | The behaviour |
|---|---|
| **The ruling may go against them** | *farīqun minhum **muʿriḍūn*** — they turn aside |
| **The ruling will go in their favour** | *yaʾtū ilayhi **mudhʿinīn*** — they come submissively |

**The verse, that is, measures a man's attachment to a tribunal by the direction of the ruling that tribunal will give.**

**And this is an extremely objective measure, which I record as my own reading:** whether a man truly trusts an arbiter is known when the arbiter decides against him. **Everyone trusts the arbiter who decides in his favour.**

**أَذْعَنَ — root ذ-ع-ن:** the sense the lexicographers record: **to submit, to obey, to comply readily.** *Nāqatun midhʿān* — a camel easily driven.

**And the choice of the word is worth noting:** when they come, they come not **unwillingly** but **submissively.** The matter, that is, is not a reluctance; it is a calculation.

### أَفِى قُلُوبِهِم مَّرَضٌ — three possibilities, and a fourth answer

**The fiftieth verse ranges three possibilities for the cause of this stance, and then gives a fourth answer.**

| Order | Possibility | What it conveys |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *a fī qulūbihim maraḍun* | **Sickness** — a disorder in the heart |
| **2** | *ami'rtābū* | **Doubt** — irresolution |
| **3** | *am yakhāfūna an yaḥīfa'llāhu ʿalayhim* | **Fear** — anxiety at being wronged |
| **The answer** | *bal ulāʾika humu'l-ẓālimūn* | **None of these: wrongdoing** |

**And that all three possibilities are rejected is worth noting.** The first three could have served as an **excuse**: sickness is a state, doubt a condition of mind, fear a feeling. **The verse accepts none of the three and makes a fourth naming.**

**يَحِيفَ — root ح-ي-ف:** the sense the lexicographers record: **to lean to one side, to do injustice.** *Ḥayf* — wrong, injustice.

**And the place of the third possibility in the arrangement is worth noting, and I record this as my own reading:** the verse puts a ground into the mouths of the men, and then rejects it. **The text, that is, itself utters the most plausible excuse the other side could offer.**

This is a move like the style recorded in `049-hucurat.md`: the objection is not concealed; it is first said and then met.

---

## 24/51-52 — إِنَّمَا كَانَ قَوْلَ ٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ إِذَا دُعُوٓا۟ إِلَى ٱللَّهِ وَرَسُولِهِۦ لِيَحْكُمَ بَيْنَهُمْ أَن يَقُولُوا۟ سَمِعْنَا وَأَطَعْنَا وَأُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلْمُفْلِحُونَ · وَمَن يُطِعِ ٱللَّهَ وَرَسُولَهُۥ وَيَخْشَ ٱللَّهَ وَيَتَّقْهِ فَأُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلْفَآئِزُونَ

*Innamā kāna qawla'l-muʾminīna idhā duʿū ila'llāhi wa rasūlihī li-yaḥkuma baynahum an yaqūlū samiʿnā wa aṭaʿnā, wa ulāʾika humu'l-mufliḥūn · Wa man yuṭiʿi'llāha wa rasūlahū wa yakhsha'llāha wa yattaqhi fa-ulāʾika humu'l-fāʾizūn*

"When they are called to God and His Messenger, that he may judge between them, the word of the believers is only to say: 'We hear and we obey.' It is they who prosper. And whoever obeys God and His Messenger, and fears God and guards himself against Him — it is they who are the winners."

### إِنَّمَا — restriction

**إِنَّمَا is in Arabic a particle of *ḥaṣr* (restriction).** The word was analysed at `049-hucurat.md`, 49/10 and 49/15; the note there: **the particle confines the ruling to a single thing.**

**And its work here:** the word of the believers is **only** this; no other option is enumerated.

### سَمِعْنَا وَأَطَعْنَا — two verbs

**The order of the two verbs is worth noting: first *samiʿnā* (we hear), then *aṭaʿnā* (we obey).**

**And this expression is named in the Qurʾān together with its opposite:** *samiʿnā wa ʿaṣaynā* — "we hear and we disobey" (al-Baqara 2/93; al-Nisāʾ 4/46). **The same first word, two opposite second words.**

**The distinction, that is, lies not in the hearing but in what comes after the hearing.**

I record this as an observation about the word order.

**And it can be compared with the word in the forty-seventh verse:**

| Verse | What is said | What follows |
|---|---|---|
| **47** | *āmannā bi'llāhi wa bi'l-rasūli wa **aṭaʿnā*** | *thumma yatawallā* — then he turns away |
| **51** | *samiʿnā wa **aṭaʿnā*** | *ulāʾika humu'l-mufliḥūn* |

**The same word (*aṭaʿnā*) occurs in both verses.** And the difference lies not in the word but in what comes after it.

**I record this contrast as my own reading;** the two occurrences of the word stand in the text.

### ٱلْمُفْلِحُونَ and ٱلْفَآئِزُونَ — two words

**فَلَحَ — root ف-ل-ح:** analysed at the verse-ending of the thirty-first verse. **To split the soil, to till; and from there, to attain what one seeks.**

**فَازَ — root ف-و-ز:** to be delivered, to win, to reach what one wants. *Fawz* — gain, deliverance. *Mafāza* — a place of deliverance; and a desert (a usage of *tafāʾul* the lexicographers record).

**The difference between the two words is recorded in the classical sources thus:**

| Word | Root | Its picture |
|---|---|---|
| **مُفْلِح** | ف-ل-ح — to split | **What splits the soil and comes out** — at the end of a process |
| **فَائِز** | ف-و-ز — to win | **One who is delivered, who arrives** — the outcome |

**Two verses one after the other, ending with two different words.** I record this as an observation about the words.

**And in the second verse three verbs are enumerated:** *yuṭiʿ* (obedience), *yakhsha* (fear), *yattaqi* (guarding oneself) — and all three from different roots.

**خَشِيَ — root خ-ش-ي:** the sense the lexicographers record: **a fear grounded on knowledge** — the fear that arises from knowing the greatness of what is feared.

**ٱتَّقَىٰ — root و-ق-ي:** to guard oneself, to keep oneself from a thing. The root was taken up in `049-hucurat.md` as the backbone of that sūra.

**The difference between the two words is worth noting:** the one conveys a **feeling**, the other a **behaviour.**

---

## 24/53 — وَأَقْسَمُوا۟ بِٱللَّهِ جَهْدَ أَيْمَٰنِهِمْ لَئِنْ أَمَرْتَهُمْ لَيَخْرُجُنَّ قُل لَّا تُقْسِمُوا۟ طَاعَةٌ مَّعْرُوفَةٌ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ خَبِيرٌۢ بِمَا تَعْمَلُونَ

*Wa aqsamū bi'llāhi jahda aymānihim la-in amartahum la-yakhrujunn, qul lā tuqsimū, ṭāʿatun maʿrūfa, inna'llāha khabīrun bimā taʿmalūn*

"They swore by God with the utmost of their oaths that if you ordered them they would certainly go forth. Say: 'Do not swear. **A known obedience** is what is asked. God is aware of what you do.'"

### جَهْدَ أَيْمَٰنِهِمْ — "the utmost of their oaths"

**جَهْد — root ج-ه-د:** to spend strength, to exert oneself. *Jihād* and *ijtihād* are from the same root. **And *jahda aymānihim*: the utmost degree their oaths can reach.**

**The expression is a pattern repeated in the Qurʾān** (al-Anʿām 6/109; al-Naḥl 16/38; al-Nūr 24/53; Fāṭir 35/42).

**And what the places where the pattern occurs have in common is worth noting, and I give this as an observation:** in the Qurʾān the expression generally occurs together with **a warning that follows the oath.**

### قُل لَّا تُقْسِمُوا۟ — an unlooked-for answer

**Here is the most striking place in the verse.**

**A group makes an undertaking with the heaviest oath. And the answer is this: "do not swear."**

**This builds a link with the sūra's twenty-second verse.** There too there was a matter of an oath: *wa lā yaʾtali* — on the first reading, "let them not swear". **In both verses an oath is used as an instrument for fixing a behaviour, and in both verses it is rejected.**

| Verse | What the oath is for | The ruling |
|---|---|---|
| **22** | To fix **not giving** | *Lā yaʾtali* — let them not do it |
| **53** | To announce **what one will do** | *Lā tuqsimū* — do not swear |

**I record this as my own reading;** the discussion of the oath in the two verses stands in the text.

**And the logic common to the two prohibitions is this:** an oath is an attempt to put a word in the place of a deed. **In both places the sūra asks for the deed.**

### طَاعَةٌ مَّعْرُوفَةٌ — "a known obedience"

**There is disagreement in the classical commentaries over the case of the expression, and it needs transmitting:**

| Reading | The word understood | Its sense |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *Ahā ṭāʿatun maʿrūfa* | "What is asked of you is a known obedience" |
| **2** | *Ṭāʿatun maʿrūfatun amthal* | "A known obedience is more fitting" |
| **3** | *Li-takun ṭāʿatun maʿrūfa* | "Let there be a known obedience" |

**I impose no preference, and it is not binding.**

**But the word *maʿrūf* may be dwelt on.**

**مَعْرُوف — root ع-ر-ف:** to know, to recognise. **And *maʿrūf*: what is known, what is recognised; and from there "the good" — what everyone knows to be good.**

**The word is the opposite of *munkar***, and *munkar* occurred in the twenty-first verse of this sūra (*yaʾmuru bi'l-faḥshāʾi wa'l-munkar*).

| Word | Root | Its sense |
|---|---|---|
| **مُنكَر** (21) | ن-ك-ر | **Not recognised** — found strange |
| **مَعْرُوف** (53) | ع-ر-ف | **Recognised** — known |

**The two words stand facing each other in the sūra.** I record this as an observation about the word order.

**And the measure the expression carries is this, which I record as my own reading:** a *maʿrūf* obedience is an obedience **whose measure is known.** Not one exaggerated in words, but one done in fact and within known bounds.

**The verse, that is, sets what is *known* over against great oaths.**

---

## 24/54 — قُلْ أَطِيعُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ وَأَطِيعُوا۟ ٱلرَّسُولَ فَإِن تَوَلَّوْا۟ فَإِنَّمَا عَلَيْهِ مَا حُمِّلَ وَعَلَيْكُم مَّا حُمِّلْتُمْ وَإِن تُطِيعُوهُ تَهْتَدُوا۟ وَمَا عَلَى ٱلرَّسُولِ إِلَّا ٱلْبَلَٰغُ ٱلْمُبِينُ

*Qul aṭīʿu'llāha wa aṭīʿu'l-rasūl, fa-in tawallaw fa-innamā ʿalayhi mā ḥummila wa ʿalaykum mā ḥummiltum, wa in tuṭīʿūhu tahtadū, wa mā ʿala'l-rasūli illa'l-balāghu'l-mubīn*

"Say: 'Obey God, and obey the Messenger.' If you turn away, upon him is only what he has been charged with, and upon you what you have been charged with. If you obey him you will be guided. And nothing is laid upon the Messenger but plain delivery."

### حُمِّلَ — to be charged with

**Root: ح-م-ل.** Its concrete sense is **to carry a load.** *Ḥiml* — a load; *ḥamūla* — what is carried.

**The form: the passive of Form II — *ḥummila*: "it was laid upon him".**

**And the choice of the form is worth noting:** the verb is passive. **Responsibility, that is, is named not as something a man chooses for himself but as a load laid upon him.**

**And the symmetry of the sentence is worth noting:**

| Whom | What |
|---|---|
| *ʿalayhi* — **upon him** | *mā ḥummila* — what was laid upon him |
| *ʿalaykum* — **upon you** | *mā ḥummiltum* — what was laid upon you |

**The same verb is applied to both sides.** The sentence, that is, sets up no superiority; **it describes two distinct loads.**

### ٱلْبَلَٰغُ ٱلْمُبِينُ — the limit

**بَلَاغ — root ب-ل-غ:** to reach, to arrive. *Balagha* — he arrived; *balāgh* — a conveying, a delivering.

**And the expression is a note of limit repeated in the Qurʾān:** the Messenger's work is the delivering; not the outcome.

**The root ب-ي-ن occurs here again (*al-mubīn*)** and was recorded in the table of its distribution through the sūra.

---

## 24/55 — وَعَدَ ٱللَّهُ ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ مِنكُمْ وَعَمِلُوا۟ ٱلصَّٰلِحَٰتِ لَيَسْتَخْلِفَنَّهُمْ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ كَمَا ٱسْتَخْلَفَ ٱلَّذِينَ مِن قَبْلِهِمْ وَلَيُمَكِّنَنَّ لَهُمْ دِينَهُمُ ٱلَّذِى ٱرْتَضَىٰ لَهُمْ وَلَيُبَدِّلَنَّهُم مِّنۢ بَعْدِ خَوْفِهِمْ أَمْنًا يَعْبُدُونَنِى لَا يُشْرِكُونَ بِى شَيْـًٔا وَمَن كَفَرَ بَعْدَ ذَٰلِكَ فَأُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلْفَٰسِقُونَ

*Waʿada'llāhu'lladhīna āmanū minkum wa ʿamilu'l-ṣāliḥāti la-yastakhlifannahum fi'l-arḍi kama'stakhlafa'lladhīna min qablihim wa la-yumakkinanna lahum dīnahumu'lladhi'rtaḍā lahum wa la-yubaddilannahum min baʿdi khawfihim amnā, yaʿbudūnanī lā yushrikūna bī shayʾā, wa man kafara baʿda dhālika fa-ulāʾika humu'l-fāsiqūn*

"God has promised those of you who believe and do righteous works that He will surely make them successors in the earth, as He made those before them successors; and that He will surely establish for them their religion, which He has approved for them; and that He will surely give them, after their fear, safety. They will serve Me, associating nothing with Me. And whoever denies after that — it is they who are the transgressors."

### A note of method

**This verse has been much cited in political arguments through history and today. Those arguments are not entered in this section.**

**The rule of the method (`STYLE.md`) is plain: no side is taken in current politics.** What will be treated is the verse's own wording, its conditions and its arrangement.

### The conditions the verse itself lays down

**The most striking side of the verse is how many qualifications it sets upon the promise. And these qualifications can be counted from the text:**

| Qualification | Expression | What it limits |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *alladhīna āmanū* | **Faith** |
| **2** | *wa ʿamilu'l-ṣāliḥāt* | **Deed** — faith alone is not counted sufficient |
| **3** | *minkum* | From **within** the community addressed |
| **4** | *yaʿbudūnanī* | **Service** — the state that follows the promise |
| **5** | *lā yushrikūna bī shayʾā* | **Not associating** |
| **6** | *wa man kafara baʿda dhālika…* | **A condition is bound to what follows as well** |

**Six qualifications, in a single sentence of promise.**

**And this is the verse's most important observation on the arrangement, and I record it as my own reading:** the promise is given to a community not for its **identity** but for its **attributes.** And the attributes are enumerated one by one.

**This agrees with the measure recorded in `049-hucurat.md` and set down in the method:** *"what the verse describes are attributes; whoever bears that attribute is included in it."*

### ٱسْتَخْلَفَ — to make successors

**Root: خ-ل-ف.** Its concrete sense is **to come behind, to come after.** *Khalf* — what is behind; *khalaf* — one who comes after; *ikhtilāf* — a coming one after another, and from there, difference.

**And *istikhlāf*: to bring one after another, to set him in another's place.**

**The idea of "coming after" in the root of the word is worth noting, and I give this as an observation about the root:** *khalīfa*, in the root of the word, conveys **an order of succession** — one who comes after another. **And the verse says this outright: *kama'stakhlafa'lladhīna min qablihim* — "as He made those before them successors".**

**The verse, that is, records within itself that what it gives has been given before as well, and that it is part of a succession.**

**And this is a thing that must be recorded:** to be part of a succession means not to be permanent. One who comes after implies that after him too there will be a successor.

**I record this as my own reading;** the expression *kama'stakhlafa* is the wording of the text.

### مَكَّنَ — to establish firmly

**Root: م-ك-ن.** *Makān* (a place) is from the same root. **And *tamkīn*: to give a thing place and firmness, to settle it.**

**The object of the word is worth noting: *la-yumakkinanna lahum dīnahum* — "He will surely establish for them their religion".**

**What is established firmly, that is, is not a state, a power or a territory — but a *religion*.** This is read from the wording of the text.

**And the qualification *alladhi'rtaḍā lahum*: "which He has approved for them".** The subject of the word is God; the party who approves is thus stated as well.

### مِّنۢ بَعْدِ خَوْفِهِمْ أَمْنًا — safety

**أَمْن — root أ-م-ن.** And this is the same root as the word *īmān*. The analysis of the root was made at `049-hucurat.md`, 49/14; the note there: **at the centre of the root stand trust, safety, the lifting of fear.**

**And the ring within the verse's arrangement is worth noting:** the verse begins with *alladhīna **āmanū*** and gives a promise with *amnā*. **The same root at the two ends of the verse: the one a condition, the other an outcome.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order.**

### The verse's place in the block

**That the verse comes after the forty-seventh to the fifty-fourth is worth noting.**

**Those verses described a stance in which there was a distance between what was said and what was done.** And in the fifty-third verse great oaths were rejected and a *ṭāʿatun maʿrūfa* — a known obedience — was asked for.

**The fifty-fifth verse gives the counterpart of what was asked for.**

| Verse | What |
|---|---|
| **47-50** | There is a word, no deed |
| **51-52** | Word and deed together |
| **53-54** | The oath is rejected, a known obedience is asked for |
| **55** | **The promise** — on the condition of faith and deed |

**The promise, that is, comes at the end of a series and upon the conditions that series has established.**

**I record this as my own reading;** the order of the verses stands in the text.

### وَمَن كَفَرَ بَعْدَ ذَٰلِكَ فَأُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ هُمُ ٱلْفَٰسِقُونَ

**Having given the promise, the verse enters one further qualification, and the word is worth noting: *al-fāsiqūn*.**

**And the word occurred in the sūra's fourth verse too.**

| Verse | Who is *fāsiq* |
|---|---|
| **4** | One who cannot prove his charge |
| **55** | One who denies after the promise |

**The same word, at the two ends of the sūra.** The sense of the root ف-س-ق — "to come out of the skin" — was analysed at `049-hucurat.md`, 49/6: **a thing's being outside where it ought to be inside.**

**And both occurrences convey a *position*.** I record this as an observation about the word order.

---

## 24/56-57 — وَأَقِيمُوا۟ ٱلصَّلَوٰةَ وَءَاتُوا۟ ٱلزَّكَوٰةَ وَأَطِيعُوا۟ ٱلرَّسُولَ لَعَلَّكُمْ تُرْحَمُونَ · لَا تَحْسَبَنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا۟ مُعْجِزِينَ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ وَمَأْوَىٰهُمُ ٱلنَّارُ وَلَبِئْسَ ٱلْمَصِيرُ

*Wa aqīmu'l-ṣalāta wa ātu'l-zakāta wa aṭīʿu'l-rasūla laʿallakum turḥamūn · Lā taḥsabanna'lladhīna kafarū muʿjizīna fi'l-arḍ, wa maʾwāhumu'l-nār, wa la-biʾsa'l-maṣīr*

"Keep up the prayer, give the alms, and obey the Messenger, that you may be shown mercy. Do not think that those who disbelieve can thwart Him in the earth. Their refuge is the Fire; and an evil journey's end it is!"

### Three commands

**The fifty-sixth verse gives three concrete commands after the promise of the fifty-fifth: prayer, alms, obedience.**

**And this threesome occurred in the thirty-seventh verse as well:** *lā tulhīhim tijāratun wa lā bayʿun ʿan **dhikri'llāhi wa iqāmi'l-ṣalāti wa ītāʾi'l-zakāh***.

| Verse | Context |
|---|---|
| **37** | The **attribute** of the people in the houses where the lamp is |
| **56** | The **command** given after the promise |

**The same threesome, once as a description and once as a command.** I record this as an observation about the word order.

**And the verse-ending is worth noting: *laʿallakum turḥamūn* — "that you may be shown mercy".** The same verse-ending occurred at `049-hucurat.md`, 49/10.

### لَا تَحْسَبَنَّ — the fourth *ḥasiba*

**The root ح-س-ب occurs for the fourth time in the sūra**, and the table given at the thirty-ninth verse is completed here. **All four occurrences take as their subject a wrong appraisal.**

**مُعْجِز — root ع-ج-ز:** to be powerless, to be unable. *ʿAjz* — powerlessness; *muʿjiz* — one who renders another powerless.

**And the form of the word is worth noting:** *muʿjizīn* does not mean "those who escape" — it means **"those who render powerless".** What is supposed, that is, is not an escape but **a comparison of powers.**

**مَأْوَىٰ — root أ-و-ي:** to take refuge, to shelter. *Maʾwā* — a place of refuge.

**And the placing of the word here is a contradiction in terms:** *maʾwā* is a word for a **refuge**; and in the verse the refuge is said to be the Fire.

I record this as an observation about the word.

---

## 24/58 — يَٰٓأَيُّهَا ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ لِيَسْتَـْٔذِنكُمُ ٱلَّذِينَ مَلَكَتْ أَيْمَٰنُكُمْ وَٱلَّذِينَ لَمْ يَبْلُغُوا۟ ٱلْحُلُمَ مِنكُمْ ثَلَٰثَ مَرَّٰتٍ مِّن قَبْلِ صَلَوٰةِ ٱلْفَجْرِ وَحِينَ تَضَعُونَ ثِيَابَكُم مِّنَ ٱلظَّهِيرَةِ وَمِنۢ بَعْدِ صَلَوٰةِ ٱلْعِشَآءِ ثَلَٰثُ عَوْرَٰتٍ لَّكُمْ لَيْسَ عَلَيْكُمْ وَلَا عَلَيْهِمْ جُنَاحٌۢ بَعْدَهُنَّ طَوَّٰفُونَ عَلَيْكُم بَعْضُكُمْ عَلَىٰ بَعْضٍ كَذَٰلِكَ يُبَيِّنُ ٱللَّهُ لَكُمُ ٱلْءَايَٰتِ وَٱللَّهُ عَلِيمٌ حَكِيمٌ

*Yā ayyuhā'lladhīna āmanū li-yastaʾdhinkumu'lladhīna malakat aymānukum wa'lladhīna lam yablughu'l-ḥuluma minkum thalātha marrāt, min qabli ṣalāti'l-fajri wa ḥīna taḍaʿūna thiyābakum mina'l-ẓahīrati wa min baʿdi ṣalāti'l-ʿishāʾ, thalāthu ʿawrātin lakum, laysa ʿalaykum wa lā ʿalayhim junāḥun baʿdahunn, ṭawwāfūna ʿalaykum baʿḍukum ʿalā baʿḍ, ka-dhālika yubayyinu'llāhu lakumu'l-āyāt, wa'llāhu ʿalīmun ḥakīm*

"O you who believe, let those your right hands possess, and those among you who have not yet reached puberty, ask leave of you at three times: before the dawn prayer, when you lay aside your garments in the heat of noon, and after the night prayer. These are three times of nakedness for you. Beyond these there is no fault upon you or upon them; you go about among one another. Thus God makes His verses clear to you. God is knowing, wise."

### The sūra's second discussion of asking leave

**The twenty-seventh verse regulated the outer door of the house. The fifty-eighth regulates the inside of the house.**

**And this is one of the most striking repetitions in the sūra's structure:**

| | 24/27-29 | 24/58-59 |
|---|---|---|
| **Whose entering** | **One coming from outside** | **One living in the house** |
| **The door** | The outer door of the house | **The door of a room** |
| **The time** | **Always** | **Three definite times** |
| **The verb** | *tastaʾnisū*, *yuʾdhana* | *li-yastaʾdhin*, *fa'l-yastaʾdhinū* |
| **In between** | — | **The whole section of light (30-57)** |

**The sūra takes up the matter of asking leave twice, and sets a section of light between them.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order;** the places of the verses stand in the text.

### The three times, and the measure by which they are chosen

**The most striking side of the verse is what the three chosen times have in common:**

| Time | Expression | What happens |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *min qabli ṣalāti'l-fajr* | **Before the dawn prayer** — the time of rising from sleep |
| **2** | *ḥīna taḍaʿūna thiyābakum mina'l-ẓahīra* | **In the heat of noon** — the time of resting, of laying aside one's clothes |
| **3** | *min baʿdi ṣalāti'l-ʿishāʾ* | **After the night prayer** — the time of going to bed |

**What the three have in common: times when a man may not be dressed.**

**And the verse says this itself: *thalāthu ʿawrātin lakum* — "three times of *ʿawra* for you".**

**ظَهِيرَة — root ظ-ه-ر:** noon, the hottest hour of the day. The word has to do with the sense of "coming out on top" in the root: the time when the sun stands highest.

**حُلُم — root ح-ل-م:** a dream; and puberty. *Ḥulm* — a dream. **And the word's sense of puberty is a usage the lexicographers record.**

**From the same root comes *ḥilm* (forbearance, self-restraint) too**, and it was named at `049-hucurat.md`, 49/6, as the opposite of *jahl*.

### عَوْرَٰت — root ع-و-ر

**Root: ع-و-ر.** This root was analysed at `033-ahzab.md`, 33/13; I rely on that treatment. The note there:

> **عَوْرَة — root ع-و-ر.** And the field of sense of the root is wide; what joins all of it is **a lack, an opening, an unprotectedness.**

And the derivatives given there: *aʿwar* (one blind in one eye — lacking on one side), *ʿāriya* (what is borrowed — not owned permanently).

**And the use of the word here is worth noting and is striking: the verse uses *ʿawra* of a time.**

*Thalāthu **ʿawrātin** lakum* — "three times of *ʿawra*".

**The word, that is, is used here not of a region of the body but of three moments of the day.**

**This agrees exactly with the root sense of the word, and I record this as my own reading:** at the centre of the root stands "unprotectedness". **And a man is unprotected at three times of the day.** The word is used as the name not of a place but of **a state.**

**And this usage may be compared with *ʿawrāti'l-nisāʾ* in the thirty-first verse:**

| Verse | *ʿAwra* | Of what |
|---|---|---|
| **31** | *ʿawrāti'l-nisāʾ* | Of a **private domain** |
| **58** | *thalāthu ʿawrātin lakum* | Of a **time** |

**The same word, on two different planes.** I record this as an observation about the word order.

### طَوَّٰفُونَ عَلَيْكُم — "you go about among one another"

**This is the most human clause in the verse, and its place in the arrangement is worth noting.**

**Root: ط-و-ف.** The same root as *ṭāʾifa* in the second verse: to turn, to go round about. *Ṭawāf* is from the same root.

**And *ṭawwāfūn* is an intensive form: "those who go about a great deal".**

**The function of the clause is this, and I record it as my own reading:** immediately after bringing in a restriction, the verse explains **why the restriction is confined to three times.**

**The reason is a fact: people living in the same house pass one another continually.** To ask leave at every passing would make life impossible.

**And the verse names this not as *an excuse* but as a normal condition:** *baʿḍukum ʿalā baʿḍ* — "one of you beside another".

**The provision, that is, is measured so as not to make life hard.**

**And this is on the same line as the "note of ease" recorded at `002-bakara.md`, 2/185.**

### What this says to the present

**One. That privacy exists within the house as well.**

The most striking side of the verse is that it places privacy not only **outside** the house but **inside** it too.

The twenty-seventh verse regulated the entering of another's house. **The fifty-eighth regulates the privacy of people living in their own house, with their own family, in respect of one another.**

**And the addressees of the provision are worth noting: the servants and the children.** That is, those within the house least able to object.

**Two. And how the measure is built.**

The verse builds privacy not as a list of prohibitions but as **three times.** And what the three times have in common is that a man has not made himself ready.

**Its counterpart today is a measure that can be drawn from the wording of the verse:** there are moments in which a man has not made himself ready, and those moments are guarded. **And those moments are not the whole day — they are countable.**

**Three. That the limit of the provision is set as well.**

*Laysa ʿalaykum wa lā ʿalayhim junāḥun baʿdahunn* — the time outside the restriction is expressly left free.

**This is the sūra's general character, and it was recorded at the twenty-ninth verse:** beside every rule, the place where the rule does not apply is written too.

---

## 24/59 — وَإِذَا بَلَغَ ٱلْأَطْفَٰلُ مِنكُمُ ٱلْحُلُمَ فَلْيَسْتَـْٔذِنُوا۟ كَمَا ٱسْتَـْٔذَنَ ٱلَّذِينَ مِن قَبْلِهِمْ كَذَٰلِكَ يُبَيِّنُ ٱللَّهُ لَكُمْ ءَايَٰتِهِۦ وَٱللَّهُ عَلِيمٌ حَكِيمٌ

*Wa idhā balagha'l-aṭfālu minkumu'l-ḥuluma fa'l-yastaʾdhinū kama'staʾdhana'lladhīna min qablihim, ka-dhālika yubayyinu'llāhu lakum āyātih, wa'llāhu ʿalīmun ḥakīm*

"And when your children reach puberty, let them ask leave as those before them asked leave. Thus God makes His verses clear to you. God is knowing, wise."

### The widening of the restriction

**The fifty-eighth verse regulated the asking of leave by children at three times. The fifty-ninth announces that at a certain age this becomes general.**

| The case | Leave |
|---|---|
| **Before puberty** | **At three times** |
| **After puberty** | **Always** — subject to the general ruling of 24/27 |

**And the qualification *kama'staʾdhana'lladhīna min qablihim* — "as those before them asked leave" — is worth noting:** the verse does not lay down a new ruling; **it brings them into a ruling that already exists.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order.**

### The verse-endings of the two verses

**The fifty-eighth and fifty-ninth verses end with the same verse-ending, and there is a small difference between the two:**

| Verse | Verse-ending |
|---|---|
| **58** | *ka-dhālika yubayyinu'llāhu lakumu'l-**āyāt*** |
| **59** | *ka-dhālika yubayyinu'llāhu lakum **āyātih*** |

**The one with the definite article (*al-āyāt*), the other with a genitive (*āyātih* — "His verses").**

**The difference is small and I build no claim of meaning upon it;** I record it only because it stands in the text.

**And both these occurrences were counted in the table of the root ب-ي-ن's distribution through the sūra.**

---

## 24/60 — وَٱلْقَوَٰعِدُ مِنَ ٱلنِّسَآءِ ٱلَّٰتِى لَا يَرْجُونَ نِكَاحًا فَلَيْسَ عَلَيْهِنَّ جُنَاحٌ أَن يَضَعْنَ ثِيَابَهُنَّ غَيْرَ مُتَبَرِّجَٰتٍۭ بِزِينَةٍ وَأَن يَسْتَعْفِفْنَ خَيْرٌ لَّهُنَّ وَٱللَّهُ سَمِيعٌ عَلِيمٌ

*Wa'l-qawāʿidu mina'l-nisāʾi'llātī lā yarjūna nikāḥan fa-laysa ʿalayhinna junāḥun an yaḍaʿna thiyābahunna ghayra mutabarrijātin bi-zīna, wa an yastaʿfifna khayrun lahunn, wa'llāhu samīʿun ʿalīm*

"And as for women advanced in years who have no expectation of marriage, there is no fault in their laying aside their outer garments, so long as they do not display adornment. Yet to keep themselves modest is better for them. God is hearing, knowing."

### ٱلْقَوَٰعِد — "those who sit at rest"

**Root: ق-ع-د.** Its concrete sense is **to sit.** *Qaʿada* — he sat; *qāʿid* — one who sits; *maqʿad* — a place to sit; *qawāʿid* — foundations (the place upon which a building sits).

**And the sense of the word in this context is a usage the lexicographers record:** women advanced in years, who have now **sat down from** marriage and from bearing.
