**The word, by its root sense, conveys a *stillness*.** And the verse names this not as a lack but as a **condition.**

**And the verse adds an explanation to the word: *allātī lā yarjūna nikāḥan* — "those who have no expectation of marriage".**

**رَجَا — root ر-ج-و:** to hope, to expect. And another sense of the word: the edge, the brink of a thing (*arjāʾ*).

**The verse, that is, takes not age but expectation as its measure.** This is read from the wording of the text.

### مُتَبَرِّجَات — root ب-ر-ج

**Root: ب-ر-ج.** Its concrete sense is **to be seen, to come out, to appear.** *Burj* — a tower, a watch-place (a structure seen from afar). **And *tabarruj*: to show a thing, to display it openly.**

**The picture in the root of the word is worth noting:** a *burj* is a structure made in order to be seen from afar. **And a *mutabarrija*: one who behaves in order to be seen.**

**And the work of the qualification is this:** the verse brings in an ease, and immediately after limits the **purpose** of that ease. **The dispensation, that is, cannot be turned into a purpose of display.**

**This does the same work as the qualification *lā yaḍribna bi-arjulihinna* in the thirty-first verse:**

| Verse | The qualification | What it closes off |
|---|---|---|
| **31** | *li-yuʿlama mā yukhfīna min zīnatihinna* | The **getting round** of the rule |
| **60** | *ghayra mutabarrijātin bi-zīna* | The dispensation's **changing its purpose** |

**Both qualifications look at a *purpose*.** I record this as my own reading.

### وَأَن يَسْتَعْفِفْنَ خَيْرٌ لَّهُنَّ — a dispensation and a preference

**The last clause of the verse, having given a dispensation, states a preference: "yet … is better".**

**And this shows a distinction within the Qurʾān's language of ruling:**

| Level | Expression | What |
|---|---|---|
| **Dispensation** | *fa-laysa ʿalayhinna junāḥ* | **Permitted** |
| **Preference** | *khayrun lahunn* | **Better** |

**The two do not conflict:** a thing may be permitted and yet something else may be better.

**This distinction occurred in many places in the long legislative sections of `002-bakara.md`** — especially in the rulings on divorce and on suckling: a permission is given, and then it is said, "such-and-such is better."

**ٱسْتَعَفَّ** — root **ع-ف-ف**; analysed at the thirty-third verse. **That the word occurs twice in the sūra, for two different groups, is worth noting:**

| Verse | Who |
|---|---|
| **33** | Those who find no means of marriage |
| **60** | Those who have no expectation of marriage left |

**The same command, to two groups at the two ends.** I record this as an observation about the word order.

---

## 24/61 — لَّيْسَ عَلَى ٱلْأَعْمَىٰ حَرَجٌ وَلَا عَلَى ٱلْأَعْرَجِ حَرَجٌ وَلَا عَلَى ٱلْمَرِيضِ حَرَجٌ وَلَا عَلَىٰٓ أَنفُسِكُمْ أَن تَأْكُلُوا۟ مِنۢ بُيُوتِكُمْ أَوْ بُيُوتِ ءَابَآئِكُمْ … فَإِذَا دَخَلْتُم بُيُوتًا فَسَلِّمُوا۟ عَلَىٰٓ أَنفُسِكُمْ تَحِيَّةً مِّنْ عِندِ ٱللَّهِ مُبَٰرَكَةً طَيِّبَةً كَذَٰلِكَ يُبَيِّنُ ٱللَّهُ لَكُمُ ٱلْءَايَٰتِ لَعَلَّكُمْ تَعْقِلُونَ

*Laysa ʿala'l-aʿmā ḥarajun wa lā ʿala'l-aʿraji ḥarajun wa lā ʿala'l-marīḍi ḥarajun wa lā ʿalā anfusikum an taʾkulū min buyūtikum aw buyūti ābāʾikum aw buyūti ummahātikum aw buyūti ikhwānikum aw buyūti akhawātikum aw buyūti aʿmāmikum aw buyūti ʿammātikum aw buyūti akhwālikum aw buyūti khālātikum aw mā malaktum mafātiḥahū aw ṣadīqikum, laysa ʿalaykum junāḥun an taʾkulū jamīʿan aw ashtātā, fa-idhā dakhaltum buyūtan fa-sallimū ʿalā anfusikum taḥiyyatan min ʿindi'llāhi mubārakatan ṭayyibah, ka-dhālika yubayyinu'llāhu lakumu'l-āyāti laʿallakum taʿqilūn*

"There is no difficulty for the blind, no difficulty for the lame, no difficulty for the sick; nor is there any difficulty for yourselves: no fault in your eating from your own houses, or the houses of your fathers, or the houses of your mothers, or the houses of your brothers, or of your sisters, or of your paternal uncles, or of your paternal aunts, or of your maternal uncles, or of your maternal aunts, or the places whose keys are in your hands, or the house of your friend. Nor is there any fault in your eating together or separately. **When you enter houses, give greeting to yourselves, as a blessed and good wish of well-being from God.** Thus God makes the verses clear to you; perhaps you will use your reason."

### The verse's structure and its place in the sūra

**This verse is one of the longest in the sūra, and it does four separate things:**

| Part | Expression | What |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *laysa ʿala'l-aʿmā … ḥaraj* | The lifting of a restriction concerning **three groups** |
| **2** | *an taʾkulū min buyūtikum aw…* | **Eleven houses** are enumerated |
| **3** | *an taʾkulū jamīʿan aw ashtātā* | **Eating together or separately** left free |
| **4** | *fa-sallimū ʿalā anfusikum* | **The greeting** |

**And the verse's place in the sūra is worth noting:** in the twenty-seventh verse the sūra restricted the **entering** of houses. The sixty-first leaves **eating** in houses free.

**The sūra, that is, gives rulings in two different directions about the same place:**

| Verse | What | Direction |
|---|---|---|
| **27-29** | Entering | **Restricted** |
| **58-59** | Entering within the house | **Restricted** |
| **61** | Eating together | **Left free** |

**I record this as my own reading;** the subjects of the verses stand in the text.

### حَرَج — root ح-ر-ج

**Root: ح-ر-ج.** This root was analysed in `048-fetih.md`; I rely on that treatment. The note there:

> **The concrete sense of the root: narrowness, constriction.** *Ḥaraj* is a thing's being pressed together, becoming too narrow, becoming impossible to get out of. The concrete usage the lexicographers record is more vivid still: **حَرَجَة** — a thicket so dense with trees pressed against one another that it cannot be passed through. **In the root, that is, there is a picture of impassability.**

**And the use of the word here agrees exactly with the root sense:** the verse announces that a **constriction** is lifted concerning three groups of people.

**And the difference between *ḥaraj* and *junāḥ* is worth noting:**

| Word | Root | Its picture |
|---|---|---|
| **حَرَج** | ح-ر-ج — constriction | **Narrowness** — impassability |
| **جُنَاح** | ج-ن-ح — to incline | **Deviation** — an error of direction |

**And the verse uses both:** *ḥaraj* for the three groups, *junāḥ* for eating together. **Two different words; the lifting of two different burdens.**

I record this as an observation about the word.

### The naming of the three groups

**The verse enumerates three groups one by one: *al-aʿmā* (the blind), *al-aʿraj* (the lame), *al-marīḍ* (the sick).**

**And on why the verse names these groups, more than one explanation is transmitted in the classical commentaries:**

| Explanation | Its content |
|---|---|
| **1** | That these people shrank from being at a common table; the verse lifts the misgiving |
| **2** | That there were people who shrank from eating with them; the verse lifts that misgiving |
| **3** | That these people, being unable to join the fighting, feared they could take no share of the spoils |

**I do not impose a preference, and it is not binding.**

**Yet what the first two explanations have in common can be recorded, and I give this as my own reading:** both look at the matter of **being shut out of a table.**

**And its concrete counterpart may be put, in language that does not wound, thus:** at a common table the way a person eats is visible. **One who does not see may feel with his hand; one who walks with difficulty may come late; one who is ill may eat little, or eat differently.** And this visibility may give rise in that person to a diffidence.

**The verse lifts this diffidence with a ruling.** And the manner of the lifting is worth noting: the three groups are enumerated **one by one**, and after each of them the word *ḥarajun* is **repeated.**

**The verse, that is, does not name the three together and pass on with a single ruling — it names each of them separately, and repeats the ruling separately for each.**

**I record this as my own reading;** the repetition itself is the wording of the text, and can be counted.

**And a fourth group is added: *wa lā ʿalā anfusikum* — "nor upon yourselves".**

**This is the most important move in the word order, and I record it as my own reading:** having enumerated the three groups, the verse adds **the addressee himself** to the same sentence.

**The three groups, that is, are not left as a category apart.** The same ruling, in the same sentence, with the same word, is extended to everyone: *wa lā ʿalā **anfusikum***.

### Eleven houses

**The verse enumerates one by one the houses in which food may be eaten, and the list is worth noting:**

| No. | House |
|---|---|
| **1** | *buyūtikum* — your own houses |
| **2** | *buyūti ābāʾikum* — fathers |
| **3** | *buyūti ummahātikum* — mothers |
| **4** | *buyūti ikhwānikum* — brothers |
| **5** | *buyūti akhawātikum* — sisters |
| **6** | *buyūti aʿmāmikum* — paternal uncles |
| **7** | *buyūti ʿammātikum* — paternal aunts |
| **8** | *buyūti akhwālikum* — maternal uncles |
| **9** | *buyūti khālātikum* — maternal aunts |
| **10** | *mā malaktum mafātiḥahū* — places whose keys are in your hands |
| **11** | *ṣadīqikum* — **your friend** |

**I record two observations on the structure of the list:**

**One. In its kinship the list is symmetrical.** Father–mother, brother–sister, paternal uncle–paternal aunt, maternal uncle–maternal aunt. **Four pairs, and in each of them two sides.**

**Two. And the list does not end with kinship.** The last two items are not kin: **places whose keys are entrusted to one** and **a friend.**

**And the addition of the eleventh item to the list is the most striking place in the verse, and I record this as my own reading:**

***Ṣadīq* — root ص-د-ق: truthfulness.** The root was analysed at `049-hucurat.md`, 49/15 and 49/17. **And *ṣadīq*: one whose truthfulness has been tried, one who is trusted.**

**The verse, that is, adds to a list built upon blood a relation with no blood in it — and the name of that relation comes from the root "truthfulness".**

**And that the word *ṣadīq* comes in the singular is worth noting:** through the whole list every word was plural (*ābāʾikum*, *ummahātikum*…); the last word is singular. **The explanation the classical lexicographers record is that the word can be used for both the singular and the plural.**

### فَسَلِّمُوا۟ عَلَىٰٓ أَنفُسِكُمْ — "give greeting to yourselves"

**This is the most discussed expression in the verse, and more than one explanation is transmitted in the classical commentaries.**

| Explanation | Who *anfusakum* is | Its sense |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **The people of the house** | Greet the people of the house you enter; for they are of you |
| **2** | **One another** | The believers count one another as "themselves"; the greeting is mutual |
| **3** | **The man himself** | If there is no one in the house, a man greets himself |

**I do not impose a preference, and it is not binding.**

**Yet the use of the word *anfus* in this sūra and in the corpus gives some ground to the second explanation, and I give this as an observation about the word order.**

**The word had been used in the same office in the twelfth verse as well:**

| Verse | Expression | Verb |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Nūr 24/12** | *ẓanna … bi-**anfusihim** khayrā* | **To think** |
| **al-Nūr 24/61** | *fa-sallimū ʿalā **anfusikum*** | **To greet** |
| **al-Ḥujurāt 49/11** | *wa lā talmizū **anfusakum*** | **To find fault** |

**In all three verses the same word is used in an office that counts the members of a community as one another's "selves".**

**And the measure recorded at `049-hucurat.md`, 49/11 fits here exactly:** the note there was that the choosing of *anfusakum* as the object names the one found fault with not as "the others" but as "yourselves".

**Sūrat al-Nūr uses this word three times (6, 12, 61), and in two of them with a positive verb: good opinion (12) and greeting (61).**

**I record this as my own reading;** the places of the words and their verbs stand in the text.

### تَحِيَّةً مِّنْ عِندِ ٱللَّهِ مُبَٰرَكَةً طَيِّبَةً

**تَحِيَّة — root ح-ي-ي:** to live, life. *Ḥayāt* is from the same root. **And *taḥiyya*: a wish of long life and well-being for someone.**

**The sense in the root of the word is worth noting, and I give this as an observation about the root:** a greeting is, by its root, **a wishing of life.** In Arabic the word for greeting is a wish of "may you live".

**And three qualifiers are added:**

| Qualifier | Root | What |
|---|---|---|
| ***min ʿindi'llāh*** | — | **Its source** — from God |
| ***mubāraka*** | ب-ر-ك | **Blessed** — analysed at the thirty-fifth verse: an increase that lasts and shows continuity |
| ***ṭayyiba*** | ط-ي-ب | **Good, pure** — analysed at the twenty-sixth verse |

**And the return of two of these words within the sūra is worth noting:**

| Word | Its earlier occurrence | Here |
|---|---|---|
| ***mubāraka*** | 35 — *shajaratin mubārakatin* (the olive tree) | 61 — **the greeting** |
| ***ṭayyiba*** | 26 — *al-ṭayyibātu li'l-ṭayyibīn* | 61 — **the greeting** |

**The qualifier of the tree in the sūra's central verse and the qualifier of the greeting in its last block are the same.** I record this as an observation about the word order.

### لَعَلَّكُمْ تَعْقِلُونَ — the sūra's only verse-ending of *ʿaql*

**The verse ends with the root ع-ق-ل, and this is the only place in the sūra where that root occurs.**

**The root was analysed at `049-hucurat.md`, 49/4;** the note there: **the concrete sense of the root is to bind** — the rope that binds a camel's leg is called an *ʿiqāl*. **Reason is a bond, a power of holding; not a capacity but a brake.**

**And the setting of the verse-ending here makes an interesting relation with the content of the verse, and I record this as my own reading:**

The verse is from beginning to end a verse of **leaving free**: the difficulty is lifted from three groups, eating in eleven houses is left free, eating together or separately is left free.

**And the verse-ending comes with a word from the root "to bind".**

**The two are not opposed but complementary:** the limits set through the sūra (leave, the glance, speech) required an *ʿaql* — a bond. **And the sixty-first verse shows where the bond is *not*.**

**The verse, that is, says that knowing where the limits end requires the same faculty.**

**And this agrees with the sūra as a whole:** as was recorded at the twenty-ninth verse, beside every rule the sūra writes the place where the rule does not apply.

### What this says to the present

**One. The regulating of eating together.**

What the verse takes up is utterly concrete: **who may eat in whose house.**

And this is a community's most basic way of forming a bond. **To sit at a table is the most visible expression of a relation.**

What the verse does is to **make this bond easy**: eleven doors are counted, and the difficulty is lifted from all of them. **And an item with no blood in it is added to the list: a friend.**

**Two. And the matter of not being shut out.**

That the verse names the three groups separately and repeats the ruling for each of them may be read, in language that does not wound, thus: **the difficulty of a person who finds it hard to sit at a table is most often not physical but a worry about being seen.**

And the verse lifts this worry with a ruling. **And then it adds the addressee himself to the same sentence: *wa lā ʿalā anfusikum*.**

**The verse, that is, does not leave the three groups in a place apart.** The ruling is given not as a separate exemption — but **in the same sentence as everyone else.**

**And this is the most striking side of the verse, and it is read from the text.**

**Three. *Jamīʿan aw ashtātā* — "together or separately".**

The verse does not impose an order of the table. **It leaves both forms free.** And this is the stance repeated in the sūra's discussions of manners: a measure is set, but the form is left free.

---

## 24/62 — إِنَّمَا ٱلْمُؤْمِنُونَ ٱلَّذِينَ ءَامَنُوا۟ بِٱللَّهِ وَرَسُولِهِۦ وَإِذَا كَانُوا۟ مَعَهُۥ عَلَىٰٓ أَمْرٍ جَامِعٍ لَّمْ يَذْهَبُوا۟ حَتَّىٰ يَسْتَـْٔذِنُوهُ إِنَّ ٱلَّذِينَ يَسْتَـْٔذِنُونَكَ أُو۟لَٰٓئِكَ ٱلَّذِينَ يُؤْمِنُونَ بِٱللَّهِ وَرَسُولِهِۦ فَإِذَا ٱسْتَـْٔذَنُوكَ لِبَعْضِ شَأْنِهِمْ فَأْذَن لِّمَن شِئْتَ مِنْهُمْ وَٱسْتَغْفِرْ لَهُمُ ٱللَّهَ إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ غَفُورٌ رَّحِيمٌ

*Innama'l-muʾminūna'lladhīna āmanū bi'llāhi wa rasūlihī wa idhā kānū maʿahū ʿalā amrin jāmiʿin lam yadhhabū ḥattā yastaʾdhinūh, inna'lladhīna yastaʾdhinūnaka ulāʾika'lladhīna yuʾminūna bi'llāhi wa rasūlih, fa-idha'staʾdhanūka li-baʿḍi shaʾnihim fa'dhan li-man shiʾta minhum wa'staghfir lahumu'llāh, inna'llāha ghafūrun raḥīm*

"The believers are only those who believe in God and His Messenger and who, when they are with him upon **a matter that gathers them**, do not depart until they have asked his leave. Those who ask your leave — it is they who believe in God and His Messenger. So when they ask your leave for some affair of their own, give leave to whom you will of them, and ask God's forgiveness for them. God is forgiving, merciful."

### The sūra's third discussion of asking leave

**The sūra takes up the matter of asking leave for the third time — and this time what is at issue is not a place but an *affair*.**

| Verse | Leave for what | The limit |
|---|---|---|
| **27-29** | **To enter a house** | The door |
| **58-59** | **To enter a room** | The room door, three times |
| **62** | **To leave an affair** | **A gathering affair** |

**And the third looks in a direction different from the others: the first two ask leave to *enter*, the third to *go out*.**

**I record this as my own reading;** the verbs of the three verses stand in the text.

### أَمْرٍ جَامِعٍ — "a gathering affair"

**جَامِع — root ج-م-ع:** to gather, to bring together. *Jamāʿa*, *jumuʿa*, *jāmiʿ* are from the same root. **And *jāmiʿ*: that which gathers.**

**And the word order of the expression is worth noting: *amrin jāmiʿ* — "a gathering affair".**

**The qualifier of the affair, that is, is not "important" or "great" — but gathering.** What defines the affair is that it brings people together.

**And the word *amr* comes indefinite:** which affair it is is not said. **This makes the ruling general.**

**In the classical commentaries various examples are transmitted as to what this expression intends** — a collective consultation, preparation for war, the Friday assembly. **I do not impose a preference;** and the word's coming indefinite suggests that none of the examples exhausts the expression.

### لَّمْ يَذْهَبُوا۟ حَتَّىٰ يَسْتَـْٔذِنُوهُ — leave to depart

**And the most striking side of the verse is the measure it sets up.**

**The verse regulates not the joining of a gathering — but the leaving of it once one has joined.**

**And this is a thing worth recording, and I give it as my own reading:** in a common affair, joining is visible — it is plain who came. **Leaving may not be visible.** To go quietly out of a crowd is easier than not to come at all.

**And the sixty-third verse will say this outright: *alladhīna yatasallalūna minkum liwādhā* — "those among you who slip away by stealth".**

### The link with `049-hucurat.md`, 49/1

**This verse forms a direct pair with the first verse of Sūrat al-Ḥujurāt, and the link must be recorded.**

**What was recorded at Ḥujurāt 49/1 was this:**

> *lā tuqaddimū bayna yadayi'llāhi wa rasūlih* — do not put yourselves forward before God and His Messenger. … **the prohibition is set not upon a kind of behaviour but upon a positioning.**

**And the verses of the two sūras regulate the two ends of an order of common work:**

| | **al-Ḥujurāt 49/1** | **al-Nūr 24/62** |
|---|---|---|
| **What it regulates** | **Putting oneself forward** | **Departing** |
| **Direction** | **Forward** — before the line | **Outward** — from the assembly |
| **The verb** | *lā tuqaddimū* | *lam yadhhabū ḥattā yastaʾdhinūh* |
| **What is asked** | To wait | **To ask leave** |
| **The kind of note** | A prohibition | **A condition** |
| **Its link with faith** | An address: *yā ayyuha'lladhīna āmanū* | **A definition: *innama'l-muʾminūn*** |

**The two verses are about a person's position within a community:** the one regulates going before the line, the other going out of it.

**And the logic recorded in `049-hucurat.md` works here too:** *"when a community grows, where each stands produces a hierarchy of itself."*

**I record this comparison as my own reading;** the wording of the two verses stands in the text.

### إِنَّمَا ٱلْمُؤْمِنُونَ — a sentence of definition

**The verse opens with *innamā* and sets up a definition: "the believers are only …".**

**And the content of the definition is worth noting:** two conditions are enumerated, and the second is a **behaviour**.

| Condition | Expression | Kind |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | *āmanū bi'llāhi wa rasūlih* | **Belief** |
| **2** | *lam yadhhabū ḥattā yastaʾdhinūh* | **Behaviour** |

**And the same pattern had occurred at `049-hucurat.md`, 49/15 as well:** *innama'l-muʾminūna'lladhīna āmanū bi'llāhi wa rasūlihī thumma lam yartābū wa jāhadū…*

**Both sūras use the same pattern, and both add a behaviour to the definition of faith.**

| Sūra | The behaviour added |
|---|---|
| **al-Ḥujurāt 49/15** | Not falling into doubt, and struggling with wealth and life |
| **al-Nūr 24/62** | **Not leaving a common affair without leave** |

**And how small the behaviour in al-Nūr looks is worth noting, and I record this as my own reading:** what is added to the definition of faith is going out of an assembly with leave. **A rule of manners, that is, which looks everyday and small, is set inside a sentence of definition.**

**This agrees with the measure recorded in the Ḥujurāt section:** *"the Qurʾān builds religiousness not as a list of acts of worship but as an order of behaviour."*

### فَأْذَن لِّمَن شِئْتَ مِنْهُمْ — the leaving of it to discretion

**The verse leaves the authority to give leave to a discretion: *li-man shiʾta* — "to whom you will".**

**The rule, that is, is set upon the side that asks leave; on the side that gives leave there is no obligation.**

I record this as an observation about the word order.

### وَٱسْتَغْفِرْ لَهُمُ ٱللَّهَ — an unexpected note

**This is the most unexpected sentence in the verse.**

**The one who asks leave is, by the verse's own definition, counted a believer** (*inna'lladhīna yastaʾdhinūnaka ulāʾika'lladhīna yuʾminūn*). **And in spite of this the verse asks that forgiveness be sought for them.**

**In the classical commentaries the reason for this has been dwelt upon, and the explanations transmitted are these:**

| Explanation | Its content |
|---|---|
| **1** | To depart from a common affair carries a lack, even with leave |
| **2** | Asking forgiveness is a general note accompanying the act of giving leave |
| **3** | The note looks at a possible weakness in the intention of the one asking leave |

**I do not impose a preference, and it is not binding.**

**Yet something may be recorded in the word order, and I give this as my own reading:** the verse does not leave the granting of leave as a **closing**. Leave is given, and after it a prayer is made. **The transaction, that is, passes beyond a legal permission.**

---

## 24/63 — لَّا تَجْعَلُوا۟ دُعَآءَ ٱلرَّسُولِ بَيْنَكُمْ كَدُعَآءِ بَعْضِكُم بَعْضًا قَدْ يَعْلَمُ ٱللَّهُ ٱلَّذِينَ يَتَسَلَّلُونَ مِنكُمْ لِوَاذًا فَلْيَحْذَرِ ٱلَّذِينَ يُخَالِفُونَ عَنْ أَمْرِهِۦٓ أَن تُصِيبَهُمْ فِتْنَةٌ أَوْ يُصِيبَهُمْ عَذَابٌ أَلِيمٌ

*Lā tajʿalū duʿāʾa'l-rasūli baynakum ka-duʿāʾi baʿḍikum baʿḍā, qad yaʿlamu'llāhu'lladhīna yatasallalūna minkum liwādhā, fa'l-yaḥdhari'lladhīna yukhālifūna ʿan amrihī an tuṣībahum fitnatun aw yuṣībahum ʿadhābun alīm*

"Do not treat the Messenger's calling among you as your calling of one another. God knows those of you who slip away and steal off. Let those who go against his command beware lest a trial befall them, or a painful punishment befall them."

### دُعَآءَ ٱلرَّسُولِ — an expression on which there is disagreement

**On what the expression means, two readings are transmitted in the classical commentaries:**

| Reading | Its sense |
|---|---|
| **1** | **The Messenger's call** — when he calls, do not treat it as one of you calling another |
| **2** | **Calling out to the Messenger** — do not call out to him as you call out to one another |

**Both readings are transmitted in the classical sources; I do not impose a preference.**

**And the second reading forms a direct link with `049-hucurat.md`, 49/2:** there it was said *wa lā tajharū lahū bi'l-qawli **ka-jahri baʿḍikum li-baʿḍ*** — "do not raise your voice to him as you raise it to one another".

**The pattern of the two verses is almost the same:**

| Verse | Expression |
|---|---|
| **al-Ḥujurāt 49/2** | *ka-jahri **baʿḍikum li-baʿḍ*** |
| **al-Nūr 24/63** | *ka-duʿāʾi **baʿḍikum baʿḍā*** |

**The same structure, the same pattern of comparison.** I record this as an observation about the word order.

### يَتَسَلَّلُونَ … لِوَاذًا — to slip away

**This is the most concrete word in the verse, and the two words together set up a picture.**

**تَسَلَّلَ — root س-ل-ل:** its concrete sense is **to draw a thing slowly out of its sheath.** *Salla'l-sayfa* — he drew the sword. **And *tasallul*: to go out slowly, without being noticed.**

**The picture in the root of the word is worth noting:** a sword's coming out of its scabbard is a silent and gradual movement. **From the same root come *sulāla* (the essence of a thing, what is drawn off and filtered out of it) and, in Turkish, *sülale* — "lineage".**

**لِوَاذ — root ل-و-ذ:** to take refuge, to hide behind something. *Lādha bihī* — he took refuge with him; *malādh* — a refuge.

**And the picture the two words set up together is this:** the man goes out, and as he goes he **hides behind something** — behind another person, in the shadow of a crowd.

**The verse, that is, does not merely say "to flee": it describes the *manner* of the fleeing as well.**

**I record this as my own reading;** the concrete senses of the two roots stand in the lexicon.

**And this is the counterpart of the note in the sixty-second verse:**

| Verse | Behaviour | How |
|---|---|---|
| **62** | *yastaʾdhinūnaka* | By asking leave **openly** |
| **63** | *yatasallalūna … liwādhā* | **In secret**, hiding behind |

**The two verses set two forms of the same act — departing — face to face.** I record this as an observation about the word order.

**And the note *qad yaʿlamu'llāh*: it announces that the hiding does not work for the one who hides.**

### فِتْنَة — an affliction, or a testing?

**Root: ف-ت-ن.** This root was analysed in the discussion of `002-bakara.md`, 2/191 and in `085-buruc.md`; the note there: **to melt gold in fire and so test its purity.** From this arise both the sense of "trial" and the sense of "pressure, affliction".

**And on the sense of the word here there is disagreement in the classical commentaries:**

| Explanation | Its sense |
|---|---|
| **1** | **A worldly affliction** — a hardship that befalls one |
| **2** | **A corruption in the heart** — a slipping in a man's inward state |

**I do not impose a preference, and it is not binding.**

**And the verb is worth noting: *tuṣībahum* — root ص-و-ب.** This root was analysed at `049-hucurat.md`, 49/6; the note there: **to hit, to reach the target.** *Muṣība* is from the same root.

**And in al-Ḥujurāt the same verb was used of people acting on a false report doing harm to a community: *an tuṣībū qawman bi-jahālatin*.**

| Sūra | Who strikes | Whom |
|---|---|---|
| **al-Ḥujurāt 49/6** | **People** | A community |
| **al-Nūr 24/63** | **A trial / a punishment** | Persons |

**The same verb, in two directions.** I record this as an observation about the word order.

---

## 24/64 — أَلَآ إِنَّ لِلَّهِ مَا فِى ٱلسَّمَٰوَٰتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ قَدْ يَعْلَمُ مَآ أَنتُمْ عَلَيْهِ وَيَوْمَ يُرْجَعُونَ إِلَيْهِ فَيُنَبِّئُهُم بِمَا عَمِلُوا۟ وَٱللَّهُ بِكُلِّ شَىْءٍ عَلِيمٌ

*A-lā inna li'llāhi mā fi'l-samāwāti wa'l-arḍ, qad yaʿlamu mā antum ʿalayh, wa yawma yurjaʿūna ilayhi fa-yunabbiʾuhum bimā ʿamilū, wa'llāhu bi-kulli shayʾin ʿalīm*

"Know that to God belongs whatever is in the heavens and the earth. He knows what state you are in. On the day they are returned to Him, He will tell them what they did. God is knowing of all things."

### The sūra's seal

**The sūra closes with the word of knowing, and this agrees with its whole subject.**

**The root ع-ل-م occurs twice in the closing verse: *qad yaʿlamu* and *ʿalīm*.**

### قَدْ يَعْلَمُ مَآ أَنتُمْ عَلَيْهِ — "what state you are in"

**The expression is worth noting: *mā antum ʿalayh* — "that which you are upon".**

**The verse, that is, does not say "what you have done"; it says your state.** What is named is not an act but a **position.**

**And this agrees with a way of looking repeated through the whole sūra, and I record this as my own reading:**

Most of the things diagnosed through the sūra are not acts but **positions and inclinations**:

| Verse | The diagnosis | Kind |
|---|---|---|
| **4, 55** | *al-fāsiqūn* | **Position** — being outside the skin |
| **15** | *taḥsabūnahū hayyinā* | **An appraisal** |
| **19** | *yuḥibbūna an tashīʿa* | **An inclination** |
| **50** | *bal ulāʾika humu'l-ẓālimūn* | **Position** |
| **64** | *mā antum ʿalayh* | **State** |

**The sūra regulates acts too — but it sets its diagnoses upon positions.**

**I record this as my own reading;** the places of the words stand in the text and can be counted.

### فَيُنَبِّئُهُم بِمَا عَمِلُوا۟ — the root *nabaʾ*

**نَبَّأَ — root ن-ب-أ.** And this is the sūra's last surprise of vocabulary.

**The root was analysed in detail at `049-hucurat.md`, 49/6;** the note there: beneath the root lies the idea of **coming from one place to another, of being carried**; **a report is what is carried.** And the sentence drawn out in the Nabaʾ section: ***"A *nabaʾ* is a report upon the hearing of which something must change."***

**And at the sūra's close this root comes as a verb of telling: *fa-yunabbiʾuhum bimā ʿamilū* — "He will tell them what they did".**

**And this forms a ring with the sūra as a whole, and I record this as my own reading:**

**The sūra had been occupied with the reports people carry about one another** — reports unproven, passing from tongue to tongue, supposed to be of no weight. **And the sūra closes with an act of telling.**

| | The report within the sūra | The report at the sūra's end |
|---|---|---|
| **Its source** | Man | **God** |
| **Its ground** | *mā laysa lakum bihī ʿilm* (15) — there is no knowledge | *wa'llāhu bi-kulli shayʾin ʿalīm* (64) — **knowing of all things** |
| **Its subject** | Another's condition | ***bimā ʿamilū*** — a man's own deed |
| **Its time** | Now, in the mouth | *yawma yurjaʿūna ilayh* — the day of return |

**The sūra, that is, closes with a report that needs no verification the discussion it had opened with a report that could not be verified.**

**I record this reading as my own reading;** the roots of the two expressions and their places stand in the text.

### وَٱللَّهُ بِكُلِّ شَىْءٍ عَلِيمٌ

**The same verse-ending occurred at the end of the thirty-fifth verse too — the verse of the likeness of light.**

| Verse | Verse-ending |
|---|---|
| **35** | *wa'llāhu bi-kulli shayʾin ʿalīm* |
| **64** | *wa'llāhu bi-kulli shayʾin ʿalīm* |

**The sūra's central verse and its last verse end with the same sentence.**

**I record this as an observation about the word order;** the sameness of the two verse-endings stands in the text and can be verified.

---

## The sūra as a whole — a look back

### The sūra has a single business: where the line is to be drawn

**When the sixty-four verses have been read, it can be said that the sūra's seemingly scattered subjects meet upon a single axis.**

In Sūrat al-Nūr there are these: penal rulings, the procedure of testimony, *liʿān*, the unravelling of a slander, a call to pardon, the manners of entering a house, the glance and the covering, marriage and freedom, a likeness of light, two counter-likenesses, the signs of the universe, obedience, a promise, the manners of asking leave within the house, the manners of eating, and the manners of the assembly.

**And the whole of this list can be tied to one question: *where does a person's own domain end?***

| Block | The domain | What crosses the line |
|---|---|---|
| **2-10** | **Body and standing** — ruling and proof | An unproven imputation |
| **11-26** | **Standing** — the word spoken | A report carried from tongue to tongue |
| **27-29** | **The house** | Entering without leave |
| **30-31** | **Body and what is private** | The glance and the showing |
| **32-34** | **Impossibility** — one who cannot marry, one who has no freedom, one who is compelled | Compulsion, and the shutting of the door |
| **35-45** | *(The opposite pole: light, darkness, the universe)* | — |
| **46-57** | **The space between word and deed** | Not doing what one says |
| **58-60** | **The room and the hour** | Entering at the wrong hour |
| **61-64** | **The table and the assembly** | Shutting out, and departing in secret |

**And the gap in the middle of the table is worth noting:** between the thirty-fifth and the forty-fifth verses there is no question of a limit. **There is a light there.**

**I record this as my own reading, and its ground is the subjects of the blocks:** while the sūra is counting limits it sets a likeness in the middle of them, and that likeness is a likeness of **a guarded light.**

### Two kinds of command: holding back, and opening

**The sūra's commands are of two kinds, and the two follow one another with regularity.**

| A command of holding back | The command of opening that follows at once |
|---|---|
| **4** — A penalty for the one who imputes | **5** — The door of repentance |
| **11-21** — The condemning of the slander | **22** — **Go on giving; pardon** |
| **27-28** — Do not enter a house without leave; turn back | **29** — Uninhabited houses are free |
| **30-31** — Lower the glance | **32** — **Give in marriage** |
| **33** — Keep chaste | **33** — **Make the contract of freedom; give them property** |
| **58** — Ask leave at three times | **58** — Free at the other times |
| **61** — *(no restriction)* | **61** — **Eleven doors are opened** |
| **62-63** — Do not depart without leave | **62** — **Give leave to whom you will** |

**The sūra leaves no restriction standing alone.**

**I record this as my own reading;** the order of the verses and their content stand in the text.

### The roots that hold the sūra together

**The reason the sūra does not fall apart despite its length is that the same roots return in different blocks.**

| Root | The verses where it occurs | What it does |
|---|---|---|
| **ب-ي-ن** (to separate) | 1, 12, 18, 25, 34, 46, 54, 58, 59, 61 | **Ten times** — the name of the sūra's own work |
| **ن-و-ر** (light) | 35 (five times), 40 (twice) | The centre, and its opposite |
| **ع-ل-م** (to know) | 15, 19, 28, 29, 32, 33, 35, 41, 58, 59, 63, 64 | The presence and the absence of knowledge |
| **ش-ه-د** (testimony) | 2, 4, 6, 8, 13, 24 | The person → the declaration → **his own limbs** |
| **ب-ص-ر** (to see) | 30, 31, 37, 43, 44 | The narrowing of the glance → the taking away of sight → **those who can see** |
| **ب-ي-ت** (house) | 27, 29, 36, 61 | The house not entered → **the house raised up** → the house eaten in |
| **أ-ذ-ن** (leave) | 28, 36, 58, 59, 62 | The householder's leave → **God's leave** → leave of the assembly |
| **ز-ك-و** (to be purified) | 21, 28, 30, 37, 56 | The result of holding back |
| **ح-س-ب** (to suppose) | 11, 15, 39, 57 | **All four a wrong appraisal** |
| **ر-م-ي** (to cast) | 4, 6, 23 | The verb of imputation |
| **ح-ص-ن** (fortress) | 4, 23, 33 | What is guarded |
| **ف-س-ق** (to come out of the skin) | 4, 55 | At the sūra's two ends; announcing a **position** |
| **ط-و-ف** (to go about) | 2 (*ṭāʾifa*), 58 (*ṭawwāfūn*) | The group of witnesses / the people of the house |
| **ض-ر-ب** (to strike) | 31 (twice), 35 (*yaḍribu'l-amthāl*), 43 | In three separate offices |
| **ك-و-د** (*kāda* — to draw near) | 35, 40, 43 | **All three a drawing near to a threshold** |
| **ن-ف-س** (*anfus*) | 6, 12, 61 | An oath → good opinion → **a greeting** |
| **ق-و-ل** (speech) | 12, 15, 16, 26, 47, 51, 53, 54 | The sūra's densest root |

**The three most striking rows in the table are these:**

**One. That the root ب-ي-ن occurs ten times.** The sūra names its work "separating", and repeats it ten times. **And this is the common work of all the sūra's rulings: to separate what belongs to what.**

**Two. That all four occurrences of the root ح-س-ب announce a wrong appraisal.** The fault the sūra diagnoses is not ill will — but **a wrong weighing.**

**Three. That the three occurrences of the root ن-ف-س trace a path:** in the sixth verse the subject of an oath, in the twelfth the object of good opinion, in the sixty-first the addressee of a greeting. **The word widens from a man's own self toward the whole of the community.**

**I record this table and these readings as my own observation;** the places of the words can be counted and verified in the text.

### The verse-endings

**The endings of the sūra's verses show its character. The count below is my own observation, and can be made one by one.**

| Kind of verse-ending | How many verses | Examples |
|---|---|---|
| **A pair of divine names** | About twenty | *ghafūrun raḥīm* (5, 22, 33, 62), *ʿalīmun ḥakīm* (18, 58, 59), *raʾūfun raḥīm* (20) |
| **A note of forgiveness / mercy / repentance** | Eight | 5, 10, 20, 22, 31, 33, 56, 62 |
| **Announcing a ruling** | Six | 4 (*al-fāsiqūn*), 13 (*al-kādhibūn*), 50 (*al-ẓālimūn*), 55 (*al-fāsiqūn*) |
| **A promise** | Four | 51 (*al-mufliḥūn*), 52 (*al-fāʾizūn*), 38 |
| **A sentence of purpose** | Five | *laʿallakum tadhakkarūn* (1, 27), *laʿallakum tufliḥūn* (31), *laʿallakum turḥamūn* (56), *laʿallakum taʿqilūn* (61) |

**I record two observations:**

**One. The names *ghafūr* and *raḥīm* come together four times in the sūra**, and all four times **after a heavy ruling**: after the penalty for slander (5), in the call to pardon (22), in the prohibition of compulsion (33), in the manners of the assembly (62).

**Two. The block that carries the sūra's heaviest rulings (2-10) closes not with a ruling but with a pair of names:** *tawwābun ḥakīm* (10).

**The sūra, that is, lays down heavy rulings, and each time sets a name behind the ruling.**

### Al-Nūr and al-Ḥujurāt: two texts on one subject

**The place `049-hucurat.md` holds in this commentary as the sūra of manners was recorded. Al-Nūr is the second and longer text on the same matter.**

**A comparison of the two sūras taken together:**

| | **49. al-Ḥujurāt** | **24. al-Nūr** |
|---|---|---|
| **Length** | 18 verses | **64 verses** |
| **Subject** | The manners of behaviour | Manners **and** ruling |
| **Sanction** | **None** | **There is one** (2, 4) |
| **Procedure of proof** | None — "verify" (49/6) | **There is one** — four witnesses (24/4, 13) |
| **Kind of limit** | Voice, word, supposition, prying, backbiting | Word, body, glance, entering, hour |
| **The central word** | *ḥujurāt* — an enclosed space | *nūr* — light |
| **Structure** | A circle **narrowing** from outside inward | An alternating sequence of restriction and **opening** |
| **What stands at its centre** | *innama'l-muʾminūna ikhwa* (49/10) | **The likeness of light (24/35)** |
| **The seal** | *wa'llāhu **baṣīrun** bimā taʿmalūn* (49/18) | *wa'llāhu bi-kulli shayʾin **ʿalīm*** (24/64) |

**And the division of labour on the matters they share:**

| Matter | **al-Ḥujurāt** | **al-Nūr** |
|---|---|---|
| **The verifying of a report** | 49/6 — *fa-tabayyanū* | 24/12-13 — **if there are not four witnesses, reject it** |
| **Supposition** | 49/12 — *ijtanibū kathīran mina'l-ẓann* | 24/12 — ***ẓanna … bi-anfusihim khayrā*** |
| **Prying** | 49/12 — *lā tajassasū* | 24/27-29, 58-59 — **the door and the hour** |
| **Spreading by tongue** | 49/12 — *lā yaghtab* | 24/15, 19 — ***talaqqī* and *shuyūʿ*** |
| **A man's claim about his own identity** | 49/14-17 | 24/47-53 — **the space between word and deed** |
| **The manners of the assembly** | 49/1-5 — **putting oneself forward, the voice, the door** | 24/62-63 — **departing, and slipping away in secret** |
| **The *anfus* pattern** | 49/11 — *lā talmizū anfusakum* | 24/12, 61 — **good opinion and the greeting** |

**What the two sūras build together is this, and I record it as my own reading:**

**Al-Ḥujurāt regulates the *form* of behaviour; al-Nūr binds the *outcome* of behaviour to a procedure.**

In al-Ḥujurāt supposition is forbidden but no penalty is set — because supposition is not visible. **In al-Nūr, on the other hand, supposition in its outward state — imputation — is bound to a procedure and to a sanction.**

**And the two sūras have the same backbone:** a word is stopped before it enters circulation. **Al-Ḥujurāt does this by waiting; al-Nūr, by closing the door.**

### What the sūra says to the present — as a whole

**What each verse says to the present has been written verse by verse; here I record only what can be said about the sūra as a whole.**

**One. The placing of the accusation itself as an act.**

The most original move in the sūra's arrangement lies between the second and the fourth verses: **two verses after a penalty is set for an act, imputing that act to someone is itself bound to a penalty.**

And the logic of this opens out in the rest of the sūra: the nineteenth verse says that what is condemned is not the act but **the wish that it be spread.**

**This is a structure that prevents a society from using its own penal order as a weapon against itself.** And this structure is built out of the text itself.

**Two. That the obligation is everywhere set upon the *receiver* and upon the one who *draws near*.**

The same choice is repeated through the sūra:

| Verse | Upon whom the obligation lies |
|---|---|
| **4** | **Upon the one who imputes** — the burden of proof |
| **12, 15** | **Upon the one who receives the report** — to reject it |
| **27** | **Upon the one who comes to the door** — to ask leave |
| **30-31** | **Upon the one who looks** — to lower the glance |
| **62** | **Upon the one who departs** — to ask leave |

**In all five places the obligation lies not upon the one who needs to be guarded — but upon the one who draws near to the line.**

**I record this as my own reading;** the addressees of the verses stand in the text.

**Three. The setting of "supposing it of no weight" at the centre.**

The sūra's sharpest diagnosis is in the fifteenth verse: *wa taḥsabūnahū hayyinan wa huwa ʿinda'llāhi ʿaẓīm.*

**And the subject of the diagnosis is not an act but *an error of weighing*.** The sūra addresses not the one who does the evil but the one who reckons the evil small.

**This is a measure worth thinking about today, and it may be put, in language that does not wound, thus:** to feel no hesitation while saying a word shows that the word is light for the one who says it. **And the verse says where the lightness comes from as well: from no one's ever seeing the sum.**

**Four. And a note: how this sūra must not be read.**

Sūrat al-Nūr is a text well suited to describing other people. Within it there are slanderers, spreaders of gossip, those who do not hold their glance, those who do not do what they say, those who slip away in secret.

**And the sūra is one of the texts that most plainly prevents this from being done:**

- **The eleventh verse:** *ʿuṣbatun **minkum*** — from among you.
- **The fourth verse:** the one who cannot prove his imputation is the party who faces the penalty.
- **The nineteenth verse:** what is condemned is **the wishing that the foul thing be spread.**
- **The twenty-sixth verse:** *ulāʾika mubarraʾūna mimmā yaqūlūn* — the section closes not by sorting people into classes but with the voiding of a word.
- **The thirty-first verse:** at the end of the rulings the address opens out to all the believers and calls them to repentance: *wa tūbū ila'llāhi jamīʿan*.
- **The twenty-first verse:** *mā zakā minkum min aḥadin abadā* — not one of you could ever have been purified on his own.

**Six verses, in six separate places, close the same door.**

**And the measure recorded in `049-hucurat.md` holds here too:** the text is an instrument of diagnosis, and the diagnosis is applied first to oneself.

### The aptness of the sūra's name

**The sūra's name is taken from the thirty-fifth verse, and the choice belongs to tradition. But I can say that the choice is apt; I record this as my own reading.**

Its ground is this: **the sūra is a text built upon the eye.**

| Verse | What the eye does |
|---|---|
| **2** | *wa'l-yashhad … ṭāʾifatun* — **it sees** |
| **4, 13** | *arbaʿati shuhadāʾ* — **it is required to have seen** |
| **15** | It says what it has not seen |
| **19** | The foulness that is **wished** to be seen |
| **24** | The limbs **bear witness** |
| **27-29** | What ought not to be seen is **not entered upon** |
| **30-31** | The glance is **lowered** |
| **35** | **A light is kindled** |
| **37** | The eyes are **turned about** |
| **40** | He **cannot even see** his hand |
| **43** | The lightning **takes away the sight** |
| **44** | ***Ulu'l-abṣār*** — those who can see |
| **58** | The **three times** that ought not to be seen |

**In thirteen places, a matter of seeing.**

**And the work the sūra does can be read from this table:** it narrows the eye's **power** (30-31), limits the eye's **authority** (4, 13, 27), shows the eye's **limits** (40, 43) — and sets a lamp in the middle of it all (35).

**The sūra, that is, tries to lessen looking and to increase seeing.**

**I record this reading as my own reading;** the places of the verses and their contents stand in the text and can be counted.

---

### Where this section does not speak with certainty

**At many points through the text definite language was deliberately not used; the reader needs to know these places.**

- **No definite date was given for the year of the sūra's revelation.** The estimates transmitted in the sources were named, and it was stated that there is disagreement.

- **Three explanations of the origin of the word *sūra*** were given in a table; **none of them was preferred.**

- **The variant reading of the word *faraḍnāhā* in 24/1** was recorded; **no reciter's name was given**, because I cannot give one with certainty.

- **The explanations of the woman's being named first in 24/2** were given in a table; **no preference was made.**

- **The juristic disagreements arising from 24/2** (the case of a married person, banishment, the way the offence is established, the number of the *ṭāʾifa*) were conveyed **in the language of transmission and in a table.** **None of them is this text's preference, and none of them is binding.** The debate of criminal law and current applications were **not entered into at all.**

- **Four readings of the kind of sentence 24/3 is** (a statement / a performative / a censure / a limited event) were given in a table; **no preference was made and no legal ruling was issued.**

- **The scope of the word *muḥṣanāt* in 24/4, and whether the ruling covers men as well,** were given as transmitted opinions; **no preference was made.**

- **What the exception in 24/5 refers back to** (the discussion of *istithnāʾ baʿda jumal* in legal theory) was given as two opinions; **no legal ruling was issued and no preference was made.**

- **In the discussion of *liʿān* in 24/6-9 only the structure and the language were treated.** **None of the juristic consequences** concerning the state of the marriage, lineage, coming together again, and refusal to swear **was conveyed**, because they are not the subject of this section.

- **Three explanations of the difference between *laʿna* and *ghaḍab* in 24/7 and 24/9** were given; **no preference was imposed.**

- **The difference of syntactic analysis in 24/8-9** was recorded; **no reciter's name was given.**

- **In the section of the *ifk* (11-26) no personal name was given, no detail of any narration was conveyed, and the disputes over who was accused were not entered into.** The reason was written plainly in the text: the verses are nameless from beginning to end, and the twenty-sixth verse closes the matter by saying *mubarraʾūna mimmā yaqūlūn*. **For this section to be written by doing the very thing the verse it treats forbids would have been an inconsistency.**

- **The variant reading *kibrahū* in 24/11** was recorded; **no reciter's name was given.**

- **Two readings of whom the expression *bi-anfusihim* in 24/12 refers to** were given; **no preference was made.**

- **Three explanations of the function of the note *ʿinda'llāh* in 24/13** were given; **no preference was made.**

- **Two readings of what the expression *fi'l-dunyā wa'l-ākhira* in 24/14 attaches to** were given; **no preference was made.**

- **The variant reading *talaqqawnahū* / *taliqūnahū* in 24/15** was given in a table; **no reciter's name was written.** The interpretation that the two readings complete each other was recorded as **my own reading.**

- **Three explanations of the function of the expression *subḥānaka* in 24/16** were given; **no preference was imposed.**

- **Two readings of the verb *yaʾtali* in 24/22** (an oath / a falling short) were given in a table; **no preference was made.** That the word is read in a different form was recorded too, and **no reciter's name was given.** On the link of the root أ-ل-و with the derivative *ālāʾ*, **it was plainly stated that the lexicographers are not agreed, and no connection was built upon it.**

- **The ways 24/26 may be understood** (words / persons / acts) were given in a table; **no preference was imposed.** And **that no ruling about persons can be drawn from the verse** was stated, on the ground of the verse's own second half (*mubarraʾūna mimmā yaqūlūn*) and of the subject of the section in which it stands.

- **Three explanations of the concrete sense of the word *istiʾnās* in 24/27** were given; **no preference was imposed.**

- **Three explanations of the function of the particle *min* in 24/30** were given. **That the partitive explanation is widespread in the classical commentaries was recorded, but no preference was imposed.** The comparison made with `031-lokman.md`, 31/19 was given **not as a ruling but as an observation about structure.**

- **Four explanations of the scope of the expression *mā ẓahara minhā* in 24/31** were given in a table; **no preference was made, no legal ruling was issued, and it was recorded that none is binding.**

- **The points at which the rulings drawn from 24/31 in the schools of law diverge** were shown in a table. **None of them is this text's preference, and none of them is binding.** Current debates about covering, and politics, were **not entered into at all**, and the reader was not scolded.

- **The historical information about the use of the *khimār* in that period** was conveyed **as a note widely transmitted in the classical commentaries**; **no chain of transmission and no attribution to a source were given**, because I cannot give them with certainty.

- **That the note *in aradna taḥaṣṣunan* in 24/33 is not a condition** was given as an explanation widely transmitted in the classical commentaries, and it was further stated that both of its grounds **can be read from the text.**

- **In the discussion of *kitāba* in 24/33 no juristic detail was entered into;** the disagreements over the kind of the command, the binding force of the contract, and the amount of property to be given were passed over with no more than **a statement that they exist.**

- **Four orientations concerning the first sentence of 24/35** (the Illuminator / the light of guidance / the source of being / *tafwīḍ*) were given in a table; **none was preferred, and it was recorded that none is binding.** **The note of transcendence was set down at the very beginning and plainly:** the expression cannot be understood in the sense of a bodily light.

- **On the origin of the word *mishkāt* in 24/35** it was stated that some of the lexicographers record it as having passed in from another language; **nothing definite was said.**

- **The readings and the origin of the word *durriyy* in 24/35** were given in a table; **no reciter's name was written.**

- **Three explanations of the expression *lā sharqiyya wa lā gharbiyya* in 24/35** were given; **no preference was imposed.**

- **The discussion of the variant readings and the subject of the verb *yūqadu* in 24/35** was recorded; **no reciter's name was given and no preference was made.**

- **Three explanations of what the expression *nūrun ʿalā nūr* is** were given; **no preference was imposed.**

- **The table of the offices of the likeness's eight elements, and the reading that "the likeness tells how a light is guarded", are my own reading**, and have not been attributed to any commentator. That the elements are in the verse is the wording of the text.

- **Two readings of the subject of the sentence *qad ʿalima ṣalātahū wa tasbīḥah* in 24/41** were given; **no preference was made.**

- **Three explanations of the expression *min jibālin fīhā min baradin* in 24/43** were given; **no preference was imposed.** And **no claim of a "foretelling" of modern meteorology was drawn from this verse** — per the method (`STYLE.md`), no hunting for scientific miracles is done. The same note was set down for the expression *min māʾ* in 24/45 as well.

- **Three suppositions of the syntactic analysis of the expression *ṭāʿatun maʿrūfa* in 24/53** were given; **no preference was imposed.**

- **No current political assessment was made concerning 24/55.** The six qualifications the verse itself lays down were shown in a table, and it was recorded that the promise is bound to **attributes** and not to identity.

- **Three explanations of why the three groups in 24/61 are named** were given; **no preference was imposed.** The discussion was treated **in language that does not wound**, and without setting up a ruling about a group.

- **Three explanations of the expression *fa-sallimū ʿalā anfusikum* in 24/61** were given in a table; **no preference was imposed.** The observation resting on the use of the word *anfus* in this sūra and in `049-hucurat.md` was given **as an observation about the word order.**

- **Three explanations of the reason for the note *wa'staghfir lahumu'llāh* in 24/62** were given; **no preference was imposed.**

- **Two readings of the expression *duʿāʾa'l-rasūl* in 24/63, and two senses of the word *fitna*,** were given; **no preference was imposed.**

- **The sūra's table of structure, its web of words, the count of the verse-endings, the logic of the blocks, the sequence of "holding back / opening", the weave of the eye, and the comparison with al-Ḥujurāt — all of these were recorded as my own reading and observation.** The data they rest upon (the places of the words, the repetitions of roots, the text of the verse-endings) stand in the text and can be counted; the interpretations drawn from them are mine.

- **No claim resting on the counting of numbers or letters was used.** The repetitions of roots and the count of the verse-endings were given not as a claim of hidden meaning but **as an observation of style.**

- **No wholesale ruling was set up about any ethnic, national or religious group.** What the sūra describes are **attributes**; whoever bears that attribute is included in it.

- **No side was taken in any current political or social debate.** This rule was stated again, in particular, in the discussions of 24/2, 24/30-31 and 24/55.

- **No fabricated transmission, ḥadīth or narration was used.** Where I was not certain, language that plainly announces the uncertainty was used — "it is transmitted in the classical sources", "what the lexicographers record", "it is widely transmitted".
